\gamma(\lambda^{*})\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}T_{-n}=\gamma(\lambda^{*})\Pi_{x}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{-n+x}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}H_{-n+x}\right)\frac{\psi(-n+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}.

By Lemma
2.1
,
\gamma(\lambda^{*})=\lambda^{*}\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda^{*})
.
Thus, using (
2.7
) and (
2.19
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma(\lambda^{*})\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}T_{-n}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{*}\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda^{*})\frac{\Pi_{x}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{-n+x}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}H_{-n+x}\right)}{\Pi_{x}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{-n+x}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{*}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(-n+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(-n+x,\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda^{*})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t>M\ \textup{i.o.}\right)\geq\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\ln\psi(\Xi_{T_{-n}},\lambda^{*})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})T_{-n}>M\ \textup{i.o.}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})\frac{T_{-n}-\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}T_{-n}}{\sqrt{n}}<\frac{\ln\psi(-n+x,\lambda)-\gamma(\lambda^{*})\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}T_{-n}-M}{\sqrt{n}}\ \textup{i.o.}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})\frac{T_{-n}-\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}T_{-n}}{\sqrt{n}}<\frac{\ln\psi(-n+x,\lambda^{*})-\lambda^{*}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(-n+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(-n+x,\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}\right)-M}{\sqrt{n}}\ \textup{i.o.}\right).

Thanks to our choice of
\Omega_{2}
, there exists a subsequence

\{n_{k}=n_{k}(\omega),k\geq 1\}

such that for all
\omega\in\Omega_{2},

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\ln\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})-\lambda^{*}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}\right)-M}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\geq 0,

so

\inf_{k\geq 1}\frac{\ln\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})-\lambda^{*}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}\right)-M}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}>-\infty.

Together with Remark
2.9
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda^{*})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t>M\ \textup{i.o.}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})\frac{T_{-n_{k}}-\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}T_{-n_{k}}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}<\frac{\ln\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})-\lambda^{*}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(-n_{k}+x,\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda^{*}}(x,\lambda^{*})}{\psi(x,\lambda^{*})}\right)-M}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}\ \textup{i.o.}\right)

\displaystyle=1.

Letting
M\to\infty
,

\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}\left(\limsup_{t\to\infty}\left(\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda^{*})-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t\right)=+\infty\right)=1,

this implies
\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(\lambda^{*})=+\infty,\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda^{*}}
-a.s. or equivalently,
W_{\infty}(\lambda^{*})=0,\ \mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
-a.s..

(ii) When
|\lambda|\in(\rho,\lambda^{*})
, by Lemma
2.1
,
\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)-\gamma(\lambda)<0
.
Let
O
be the law of the offspring of the spine particle, that is,
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(O=k)=kp_{k}/m
for
k=1,2,...
.
If
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(k\ln k)p_{k}=+\infty
,
then

\infty=\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\frac{\ln^{+}O}{M}\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\ln^{+}O>yM)\mathrm{d}y\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\ln^{+}O>kM),\quad M>0.

Let
\mu_{k}
be the time of the
k
th fission of the spine and
O_{k}
be the number of offspring of the spine at time
\mu_{k}
. Then by the Borel-Cantelli lemma and the arbitrariness of
M
, we have

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{\ln O_{k}}{k}=+\infty,\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\textup{-a.s.}

Note that

\widetilde{W}_{\mu_{k}}\geq(O_{k}-1)e^{-\gamma(\lambda)\mu_{k}}\psi(\Xi_{\mu_{k}},\lambda)=\exp\left\{\left(\frac{\ln^{+}(O_{k}-1)}{\mu_{k}}+\frac{\ln\psi(\Xi_{\mu_{k}},\lambda)}{\mu_{k}}-\gamma(\lambda)\right)\mu_{k}\right\}.

Since
(m-1)\xi(x)\geq(m-1)\textup{ei}
, we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\mu_{k}\leq t)=\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(N_{t}\geq k)\geq\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\tilde{N}_{t}\geq k)=\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\tilde{\mu}_{k}\leq t),

where, given the trajectory of
\Xi
,
N_{t}
is a Poisson process with rate
(m-1)\xi(\Xi_{t})
and
\widetilde{N}_{t}
is a Poisson process with rate
(m-1)\textup{ei}
,

\tilde{\mu}_{k}:=\inf\{t:\widetilde{N}_{t}=k\}
.
We use a coupling of
(N_{t},\widetilde{N}_{t})
such that
N_{t}-\tilde{N}_{t}
is a Poisson process with rate
(m-1)\left(\xi(\Xi_{t})-\textup{ei}\right)
and is independent of
\tilde{N}_{t}
. So
\mu_{k}\leq\tilde{\mu}_{k}
.
Since
k/\tilde{\mu}_{k}\to\left((m-1)\textup{ei}\right),\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s., we have
\liminf_{k\to\infty}k/\mu_{k}\geq\left((m-1)\textup{ei}\right),\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.. Similarly we also have
\limsup_{k\to\infty}k/\mu_{k}\leq\left((m-1)\textup{es}\right),\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.. Thus,
\mu_{k}\to\infty\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s. and

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\widetilde{W}_{\mu_{k}}\geq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\exp\left\{\left(\frac{\ln^{+}(O_{k}-1)}{k}\frac{k}{\mu_{k}}+\frac{\ln\psi(\Xi_{\mu_{k}},\lambda)}{\mu_{k}}-\gamma(\lambda)\right)\mu_{k}\right\}=+\infty.

If
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(k\ln k)p_{k}<+\infty
, then for any
c>0
,

\infty>\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\frac{\ln^{+}O}{c}\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\ln^{+}O>yc)\mathrm{d}y\geq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}(\ln^{+}O>kc).

Since
c
is arbitrary, we have

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{\ln O_{k}}{k}=0,\quad\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\textup{-a.s.}

Define
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
to be the
\sigma
-field of the genealogy along the spine,
then we have

\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)\Big|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\right)=\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)e^{-\gamma(\lambda)t}+\sum_{k=1}^{N_{t}-1}(O_{k}-1)\psi(\Xi_{\mu_{k}},\lambda)e^{-\gamma(\lambda)\mu_{k}}.

Here
O_{k},\mu_{k}
and
N_{t}

have the same meaning as in the case
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(k\ln k)p_{k}=+\infty.

Then,
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.,

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{\ln(O_{k}-1)}{\mu_{k}}=\limsup_{k\to\infty}\frac{\ln(O_{k}-1)}{k}\frac{k}{\mu_{k}}=0.

Note that
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)}{t}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)}{\Xi_{t}}\frac{\Xi_{t}}{t}=\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)<\gamma(\lambda),

and thus

\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\ln\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)}{t}<\frac{\gamma(\lambda)+\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)}{2},

which implies

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(\lambda)+\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)t/2\right\}=0.

So there exists a
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s. finite random variable

\eta

such that for all
k
and
t
,

\ln(O_{k}-1)\leq\eta+\frac{\gamma(\lambda)-\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)}{4}\mu_{k},\ \textup{and}\ \psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(\lambda)+\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)t/2\right\}\leq\eta.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)\Big|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\right)=\psi(\Xi_{t},\lambda)e^{-\gamma(\lambda)t}+\sum_{k=1}^{N_{t}-1}(O_{k}-1)\psi(\Xi_{\mu_{k}},\lambda)e^{-\gamma(\lambda)\mu_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(\lambda)-\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)t/2\right\}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\eta e^{\eta}\exp\left\{\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(\lambda)-\lambda\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)\mu_{k}/4\right\}\right\}<\infty,

which implies
\limsup_{t\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\right)<\infty,\ \widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.
Fatou’s lemma for conditional probability implies that
\liminf_{t\to\infty}\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)<\infty
,
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.
Since
\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)^{-1}
is a non-negative supermartingale under
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
, we have
\lim_{t\to\infty}\widetilde{W}_{t}(\lambda)=\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(\lambda)<\infty
,

\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
-a.s.
Thus we have shown that
when
\lambda\in(\rho,\lambda^{*})
and
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(k\ln k)p_{k}<\infty
,
W_{t}(\lambda)
converges
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
-a.s. and in
L^{1}
to
W_{\infty}(\lambda)
.

\Box

Proof of Corollary
1.4
:

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists constant
c_{\epsilon}>0

such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\psi(x,-\lambda^{*})\geq c_{\epsilon}e^{\lambda^{*}x-\epsilon|x|}.

Taking
\lambda=-\lambda^{*}
and using Theorem
1.3
,
we have,
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
-a.s.,

0=W_{\infty}(-\lambda^{*})\geq\limsup_{t\to\infty}\psi(M_{t},-\lambda^{*})e^{-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t}\geq c_{\epsilon}\limsup_{t\to\infty}\exp\left\{\lambda^{*}M_{t}-\epsilon|M_{t}|-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t\right\}\geq 0.

Thus,
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
-a.s.,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\lambda^{*}M_{t}-\epsilon|M_{t}|-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t=-\infty,

which implies that

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{\lambda^{*}M_{t}-\epsilon|M_{t}|}{t}\leq\gamma(\lambda^{*}),\quad\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\textup{-a.s.}

(3.1)

When
\lambda\in(\rho,\lambda^{*})
, we will first show that for any
\omega\in\Omega_{2}
,

\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
and
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
are mutually absolutely continuous.
Define

q(x,\lambda,\omega):=\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(W_{\infty}(\lambda)=0\right).

We only need to prove that

q(x,\lambda,\omega)=0,\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}.

(3.2)

By the branching property, for
s<t
, we have

W_{t}(\lambda)=e^{\gamma(\lambda)s}\sum_{\nu\in N(s)}W_{t-s}^{\nu}(\lambda).

Here conditioned on

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
,
\{W_{t-s}^{\nu}(\lambda),\nu\in N(s)\}

are independent. Letting
t\to\infty
, we get

q(x,\lambda,\omega)=\mathrm{E}_{x}^{\xi}\left(\prod_{\nu\in N(s)}q(X_{s}^{\nu},\lambda,\omega)\right).

(3.3)

By the branching property and the Markov property,
\left\{\prod_{\nu\in N(s)}q(X_{s}^{\nu},\lambda,\omega)\right\}_{s\geq 0}
is a bounded martingale under
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
. Since
W_{\infty}(\lambda)
is the
L^{1}
limit of
W_{t}(\lambda)
,
q(x,\lambda,\omega)<1
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}.
By (
1.5
),
q(\cdot,\lambda,\omega)
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle q_{t}(x,\lambda,\omega)=\frac{1}{2}q_{xx}(x,\lambda,\omega)+\xi(x)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}q^{k}(x,\lambda,\omega)-q(x,\lambda,\omega)\right).

(3.4)

By the Feynman-Kac formula, we have

q(x,\lambda,\omega)=\Pi_{x}\left(q(B_{t},\lambda,\omega)\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\xi(B_{s})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}q^{k-1}(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)-1\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right).

Let
A(\varepsilon):=\{x:q(x,\lambda,\omega)\leq 1-\varepsilon\}
, then

\displaystyle q(x,\lambda,\omega)\leq\Pi_{x}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\xi(B_{s})\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}q^{k-1}(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)-1\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)

(3.5)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{x}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}\textup{ei}(1-p_{1})\left(q(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)-1\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)\leq\Pi_{x}\left(exp\left\{-\varepsilon\textup{ei}(1-p_{1})\int_{0}^{t}1_{A(\varepsilon)}(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right),

(3.6)

where in the second inequality, we used the domination
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}p_{k}q^{k-1}(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)-1\right)\leq p_{1}+(\sum^{\infty}_{k=2}p_{i})q(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)-1=(p_{1}-1)+(1-p_{1})q(B_{s},\lambda,\omega)
.
Let
m
be the Lebesgue measure in
\mathbb{R}
. Note that if
m\left(A(\varepsilon)\right)>0
, then as
t\to\infty
,

\int_{0}^{t}1_{A(\varepsilon)}(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\to+\infty,\ \Pi_{x}\textup{-a.s.}

Since
q(x,\lambda,\omega)<1
, we can find

\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\omega)>0

such that
m\left(A(\varepsilon)\right)>0.

Letting
t\to\infty
in (
3.5
),
we obtain (
3.2
), and
thus
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}
and
\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
are mutually absolute continuous.
Noticing that

\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{\widetilde{M}_{t}}{t}\geq\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)\geq\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}_{x}^{\xi,\lambda}\left(\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{\Xi_{t}}{t}\geq\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)=1,

we have

\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{M_{t}}{t}\geq\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\right)=1.

Let
\lambda\uparrow\lambda^{*}
we get that

\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{M_{t}}{t}\geq v^{*}\right)=1.

(3.7)

(H2)
implies that
\lambda^{*}>\rho
and
\gamma(\lambda^{*})>(m-1)\textup{es}.
By Lemma
2.3
(4),
\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda)\uparrow\gamma^{\prime}(\lambda^{*})=v^{*}
as
\lambda\uparrow\lambda^{*}
.
In view of (
3.7
), we know that
M_{t}\to+\infty,\ \mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}
-a.s. and thus (
3.1
) can be rewritten as

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{M_{t}}{t}\leq\frac{\gamma(\lambda^{*})}{\lambda^{*}-\epsilon},\ \mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\textup{-a.s.}

Letting
\epsilon\to 0+
and using (
3.7
), we get the conclusion of the corollary.

\Box

3.2
Proof of Theorem
1.5

In this subsection,
unless explicitly mentioned otherwise, we always assume

\lambda=-\lambda^{*}
and that
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
hold.
The proof of Theorem
1.5
is inspired by

[
6
, Section 6]
and
[
8
, Section 4]
.
Since the proof is long and complicated, we
divide it into several parts.
In this subsection,

K_{1}:=K_{1}(-\lambda^{*}),\quad K_{2}:=K_{2}(-\lambda^{*})

are the two constants in Lemma
2.3
(2).

For
s\in[0,t]
and

\Gamma_{0}>0
, define

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}(s):=\sup\left\{k\in\mathbb{Z}:\ s\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t\right\}.

Later we will take
\Gamma_{0}>2C(\ell^{\prime})+1
with
C(\ell^{\prime})
being defined in Lemma
3.2
(i) below.
We claim that for any
\Gamma_{0}>0
,

\varphi_{t}(s)
is càdlàg and non-decreasing as a function of
s\in[0,t].
In fact, if
\varphi_{t}(s)=k
, then by definition,

s\in\left[\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,\,\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k+1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t\right),

(3.8)

which implies that, for any

r\in\left(s,\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k+1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t\right)
,

\varphi_{t}(r)=k.
On the other hand, suppose that
s_{n}\in[0,t]
,
s_{n}\uparrow s
, and that
\varphi_{t}(s)=k
.
If
s=\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t

, then
\varphi_{t}(s_{n})\uparrow(k-1)
, if
s>\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t
, then for
n
large enough, we have
\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t<s_{n}<s
, which implies that
\varphi_{t}(s_{n})=k.

Thus
\varphi_{t}(s)
is a càdlàg function of
s\in[0,t]
.
The monotonicity follows easily from Remark
2.4
.

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t}:=\#\Big\{\nu\in N(t):

\displaystyle X_{t}^{\nu}\geq V_{t}+2,\min_{r\in[t-1,t]}X_{r}^{\nu}\geq V_{t}-3,\ X_{s}^{\nu}<\varphi_{t}(s),\ \forall s\in\left[H_{[V_{t}]}^{\nu},t\right],

\displaystyle H_{k}^{\nu}\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k+1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,\ \forall k=1,2,...,[V_{t}]\Big\},

(3.9)

here for any
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
\nu\in N(t)
,
H^{\nu}_{y}
is the first time that the particle
\nu
hits
y
,
i.e.,

H^{\nu}_{y}=\inf\{t\geq 0:X^{\nu}_{t}=y\}
.
To prove Theorem
1.5
, we will use the following inequality:

\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{t}>0\right)\geq\frac{\left(\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{t}\right)\right)^{2}}{\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left((\mathcal{L}_{t})^{2}\right)},\quad t>0.

We will estimate the first moment of
\mathcal{L}_{t}

from below and the second moment of
\mathcal{L}_{t}
from
above.
The following lemma gives the estimate of the second moment of
\mathcal{L}_{t}
.

Lemma 3.1

There exists a non-random constant
\ell_{2}
such that for

t
large enough,

\sup_{y\in[-1,1]}\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left((\mathcal{L}_{t})^{2}\right)\leq t^{\ell_{2}}.

Proof:

Note when

\nu\in N(t)
makes a contribution to
\mathcal{L}_{t}
, we
have
X_{s}^{\nu}<\varphi_{t}(s)
for all
s\in[0,t]
. This is because that for

s\in\left[H_{1}^{\nu},H_{[V_{t}]}^{\nu}\right)
,
there exists
k\in\{2,...,[V_{t}]\}
such that
s\in\left[H_{k-1}^{\nu},H_{k}^{\nu}\right)
. Therefore,

s\geq H_{k-1}^{\nu}\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,

which implies that
\varphi_{t}(s)\geq k
. But
s<H_{k}^{\nu}
means
X_{s}^{\nu}<k
, so we get that
X_{s}^{\nu}<k\leq\varphi_{t}(s)
for

s\in\left[H_{1}^{\nu},H_{[V_{t}]}\right).

If
s\in[0,H_{1}^{\nu})
, then when
t
is large enough so that
0\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t
,

s\geq 0\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,

thus
\varphi_{t}(s)\geq k
holds for
k=1
. Thus for
s<H_{1}^{\nu}
, we have
X_{s}^{\nu}<1\leq\varphi_{t}(s)
.
In conclusion,

\mathcal{L}_{t}\leq\#\left\{\nu\in N(t):X_{t}^{\nu}\geq V_{t}+2,X_{s}^{\nu}<\varphi_{t}(s),\ \forall s\in[0,t]\right\}.

By Proposition
1.1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left((\mathcal{L}_{t})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{t}\right)+(m_{2}-m)\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{y}\bigg(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}\xi(B_{s})1_{\{B_{r}<\varphi_{t}(r),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq s\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{r}\leq\varphi_{t}(r+s),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq t-s,B_{t-s}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)\right)^{2}_{|z=B_{s}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s.

Thus, for
z<\varphi_{t}(s)
, using the monotonicity of

\psi(\cdot,-\lambda^{*})

(see Remark
2.4
) and (
1.11
), we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{r}\leq\varphi_{t}(r+s),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq t-s,B_{t-s}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{t-s}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}\frac{\psi(B_{t-s},-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(V_{t}+2,-\lambda^{*})}\right)=\frac{\psi(z,-\lambda^{*})e^{\gamma(\lambda^{*})(t-s)}}{\psi(V_{t}+2,-\lambda^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\psi\left(\varphi_{t}(s),-\lambda^{*}\right)e^{\gamma(\lambda^{*})(t-s)}}{\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*})}=\psi\left(\varphi_{t}(s),-\lambda^{*}\right)e^{-\lambda^{*}v^{*}s},

(3.10)

where in the last equality we used the fact that
e^{-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t}\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*})=1
given by (
1.18
).
Since (
3.8
) implies that for
s\in[0,t]
,

s\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(\varphi_{t}(s),-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,

the last term of (
3.2
) is bounded from above by
t^{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}
for all
z<\varphi_{t}(s),s\in[0,t]
.
Similarly as in (
3.2
), for
s=0
we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{t}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}\frac{\psi(B_{t},-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(V_{t}+2,-\lambda^{*})}\right)=\frac{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*})e^{\gamma(\lambda^{*})t}}{\psi(V_{t}+2,-\lambda^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\psi(1,-\lambda^{*})e^{\gamma(\lambda^{*})t}}{\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*})}=\psi(1,-\lambda^{*})\leq e^{K_{1}},

where in the last inequality we used (
2.26
).
Therefore we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left((\mathcal{L}_{t})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{t}\right)+(m_{2}-m)\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{y}\bigg(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}\xi(B_{s})1_{\{B_{r}<\varphi_{t}(r),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq s\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{r}\leq\varphi_{t}(r+s),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq t-s,B_{t-s}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)\right)^{2}_{|z=B_{s}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\#\left\{\nu\in N(t):X_{t}^{\nu}\geq V_{t}+2\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+t^{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}(m_{2}-m)\textup{es}\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{y}\bigg(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{r}<\varphi_{t}(r),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq s\}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Pi_{z}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t-s}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{r}\leq\varphi_{t}(r+s),\ \forall 0\leq r\leq t-s,B_{t-s}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)_{|z=B_{s}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{K_{1}}+t^{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}(m_{2}-m)\textup{es}\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(m-1)\xi(B_{r})\mathrm{d}r\right\}1_{\{B_{t}\geq V_{t}+2\}}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{K_{1}}+t^{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}+1}e^{K_{1}}(m_{2}-m)\textup{es}.

Taking
\ell_{2}>\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}+1
, we arrive at the desired conclusion.

\Box

Now we estimate

\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})
from below.
Define

\displaystyle G_{t}

\displaystyle:=\left\{H_{k}\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k+1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t,\ \forall k=1,2,...,[V_{t}]\right\};

\displaystyle A(r,t)

\displaystyle:=\left\{B_{t}\geq V_{t}+2,\min_{s\in[t-1,t]}B_{s}\geq V_{t}-3,\ B_{s}<\varphi_{t}(s),\ \forall s\in\left[r,t\right]\right\};

(3.11)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle:=K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})+2.

(3.12)

For
y\in[-1,1],
by Proposition
1.1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})=\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\};A\left(H_{[V_{t}]},t\right),G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{t}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\};A\left(H_{[V_{t}]},t\right),H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{[V_{t}]}}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\};A\left(H_{[V_{t}]},t\right),H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right).

(3.13)

By the definition of
\varphi_{t}(s)
, we know that

\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(\varphi_{t}(s),-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t\leq s=\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(V_{s},-\lambda^{*})<\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(\varphi_{t}(s)+1,-\lambda^{*})-\Gamma_{0}\ln t.

It follows from Lemma
2.3
(2) that for
s\in[0,t]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{1}}\ln t-1\leq\varphi_{t}(s)-V_{s}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}\ln t.

(3.14)

By (
2.27
), when
r\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1]
and
s\in[r,t],

0\leq V_{t}-V_{s}\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}(t-s)\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}\Gamma_{1}.

Therefore, applying
(
3.14
)
for
s\in[r,t]
, we have

\displaystyle A(r,t)\supset\left\{B_{t}\geq V_{t}+2,\min_{s\in[t-1,t]}B_{s}\geq V_{t}-3,\ B_{s}<V_{s}+\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{1}}\ln t-1,\ \forall s\in\left[r,t\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\supset\left\{B_{t}\geq V_{t}+2,\min_{s\in[t-1,t]}B_{s}\geq V_{t}-3,\ B_{s}<V_{t}+\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{1}}\ln t-1-\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}\Gamma_{1},\ \forall s\in\left[r,t\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\supset\left\{B_{t}\geq[V_{t}]+3,\min_{s\in[t-1,t]}B_{s}\geq[V_{t}]-2,\ B_{s}<[V_{t}]+\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{1}}\ln t-1-\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}\Gamma_{1},\ \forall s\in\left[r,t\right]\right\}.

Hence, when
r=H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1]
and
t
is large enough so that
\frac{\Gamma_{0}\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{1}}\ln t-1-\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}\Gamma_{1}>10
,

\displaystyle A(H_{[V_{t}]},t)\supset\left\{B_{t}\geq B_{r}+3,\min_{s\in[t-1,t]}B_{s}\geq B_{r}-2,\ B_{s}<B_{r}+10,\ \forall s\in\left[r,t\right]\right\}\Big|_{r=H_{[V_{t}]}}.

Using (
3.2
) and
the strong Markov property of Brownian motion
B
,
we have for
t
large,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}[\mathcal{L}_{t}]\geq

\displaystyle\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{[V_{t}]}}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\}F_{t};H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right),

(3.15)

where
F_{t}
is defined by

\displaystyle F_{t}:=

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(B_{t-r}\geq 3,\min_{s\in[t-r-1,t-r]}B_{s}\geq-2,\ B_{s}<10,\ \forall s\in\left[0,t-r\right]\right)_{|r=H_{[V_{t}]}}.

For
r=H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1]
,

\displaystyle F_{t}\geq\inf_{r\in[1,\Gamma_{1}]}\Pi_{0}\left(B_{r}\geq 3,\min_{s\in[r-1,r]}B_{s}\geq-2,\sup_{s\in[0,r]}B_{s}\leq 10\right)=:\Gamma_{2}>0.

Plugging the lower bound of
F_{t}
into (
3.15
),
using (
2.2
) with
\lambda=-\lambda^{*}
, for large
t
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq\Gamma_{2}\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{[V_{t}]}}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\};H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\lambda^{*}v^{*}H_{[V_{t}]}}\frac{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi([V_{t}],-\lambda^{*})};H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Gamma_{2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\lambda^{*}v^{*}(t-\Gamma_{1})}\frac{\psi(-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*})};H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Gamma_{2}e^{-\lambda^{*}v^{*}\Gamma_{1}}e^{-K_{1}}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],G_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Gamma_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{[V_{t}]}\in[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1],H_{k}\geq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t,\ 1\leq k\leq[V_{t}]\right).

(3.16)

Here in the penultimate inequality,
we used
\ln\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*})=\gamma(\lambda^{*})t
and (
2.26
) with
z=0,y=-1
, and
in the last, we used

\Gamma_{3}:=\Gamma_{2}e^{-\lambda^{*}v^{*}\Gamma_{1}}e^{-K_{1}}

and
t
is large enough so that
\lambda^{*}v^{*}\ln t>K_{1}.

To continue the estimate of
\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})
, we will consider two independent copies
of
(\Xi_{t},\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}})
.
For
y\in[-1,1]
, we enlarge the probability space and the corresponding measure
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
: let
\Xi_{t}^{j},j=1,2
be two independent copies of
(\Xi_{t},\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}})
and
and

H^{j}_{x}:=\inf\left\{t\geq 0:\ \Xi_{t}^{j}=x\right\},\quad j=1,2,x\in\mathbb{R}.

(3.17)

For
j=1,2
, define

\beta_{0}^{j}:=0,\quad\beta_{k}^{j}:=H_{k}^{j}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*}),\quad k\geq 1.

(3.18)

Note that
\lambda^{*}v^{*}t-K_{1}\leq\ln\psi([V_{t}],-\lambda^{*})\leq\lambda^{*}v^{*}t,

we have

\left[\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi([V_{t}],-\lambda^{*})+\frac{K_{1}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}-\Gamma_{1},\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi([V_{t}],-\lambda^{*})-1\right]\subset[t-\Gamma_{1},t-1].

Together with the definition of
\Gamma_{1}
in (
3.2
),
we continue the estimate of (
3.2
) and get

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq\Gamma_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{j}\in[-2,-1],\beta_{k}^{j}\geq-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t,\ 1\leq k\leq[V_{t}]\right),\,j=1,2.

(3.19)

We first claim that, under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
,
\{\beta_{k}^{1},k\geq 0\}
has independent increments.
The proof of this claim is postponed to the Appendix.
Let
\pi:=\pi_{t}
be a uniform random variable on
\{2,...,[V_{t}]-1\}
which is independent of
\Xi_{t},\Xi_{t}^{1}
and
\Xi_{t}^{2}
.
Define

\beta_{k}^{(t)}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta_{k}^{1},&1\leq k\leq\pi,\\
\beta_{\pi}^{1}+\left(\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{\pi}^{2}\right),&\pi<k\leq[V_{t}].\end{array}\right.

We claim that

\left(\beta_{k}^{(t)},k=1,...,[V_{t}];\,\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{=}}\left(\beta_{k}^{1},k=1,...,[V_{t}];\,\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\right).

(3.20)

In fact, for real numbers
\alpha_{k},k=1,...,[V_{t}],
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{(t)}\right\}\Bigg|\beta_{k}^{1},\beta_{k}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{[V_{t}]-2}\sum_{m=2}^{[V_{t}]-1}\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{m}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{m}^{2}\right)\right\}.

Thus, it suffices to show for any
2\leq m\leq[V_{t}]-1
,

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{m}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{m}^{2}\right)\right\}\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}\right\}\right).

(3.21)

Since under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
,

\beta_{k}^{j},j=1,2
, are sums of independent random variables, we know that, for
k>m,\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{m}^{1}
is independent of
\beta_{r}^{1},r\leq m
. Use this observation, the left-hand side of (
3.21
) is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{m}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{m}^{2}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\beta_{m}^{1}\right\}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{m}^{2}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\beta_{m}^{1}\right\}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{m}^{1}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{k}\beta_{k}^{1}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=m+1}^{[V_{t}]}\alpha_{k}\left(\beta_{m}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{m}^{1}\right)\right\}\right).

Hence we obtain (
3.21
), and thus the claim (
3.20
) holds.

Therefore, we have

\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq\Gamma_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{(t)}\in[-2,-1],\beta_{k}^{(t)}\geq-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t,\ 1\leq k\leq[V_{t}]\right).

(3.22)

Now we are going to give a lower bound of the right hand side of (
3.22
).
For a constant
C(\ell^{\prime})
to be specified in Lemma
3.2
(i) below, define

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 0,\ \forall 2\leq k\leq[V_{t}],\,\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq[V_{t}]^{1/4}\right\};

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}\geq 0,\ \forall 2\leq k\leq[V_{t}],\,\beta_{2}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}\geq[V_{t}]^{1/4}\right\};

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\max_{3\leq k\leq[V_{t}]}\left|\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{k-1}^{1}\right|\leq C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]\right\};

(3.23)

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\max_{3\leq k\leq[V_{t}]}\left|\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{k-1}^{2}\right|\leq C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]\right\};

(3.24)

\displaystyle I_{5}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\beta_{2}^{1}\in[-2+\beta_{J}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1},-1+\beta_{J}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}]\right\}\bigcap\left\{\pi=J\right\}.

(3.25)

Here
J:=\sup\{j\leq[V_{t}]:-2+\beta_{j}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{j}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 0\}
.
To give a lower bound of (
3.22
), we will prove that for sufficiently large
t
,

\bigcap_{n=1}^{5}I_{n}\subset\left\{\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{(t)}\in[-2,-1],\beta_{k}^{(t)}\geq-\left(\Gamma_{0}-1\right)\ln t,\ 1\leq k\leq[V_{t}]\right\}.

(3.26)

By the definition of
\beta_{k}^{(t)}
, we know that, when
\bigcap_{n=1}^{5}I_{n}
occurs, we have
\beta_{k}^{(t)}=\beta_{k}^{1}
if
1\leq k\leq\pi=J
and

\beta_{k}^{(t)}=\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{2}
if
J<k\leq[V_{t}]
. Note that on
I_{1}\cap I_{2}
we have

\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq[V_{t}]^{1/4},\qquad\beta_{2}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}\geq[V_{t}]^{1/4}.

This implies that for
t
large enough so that
[V_{t}]^{1/4}>2
,

-2+\beta_{2}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{2}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}>0\ \textup{and}\ -2+\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}<0,

which implies that

2\leq J<[V_{t}]
.
When
k\leq J
, then on
I_{1}\cap I_{3}
we have
for
k\geq 2
,

\beta_{k}^{(t)}=\beta_{k}^{1}\geq\beta_{2}^{1}=H_{2}^{1}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(2,-\lambda^{*})\geq-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(2,-\lambda^{*})\geq-\frac{2K_{1}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}

and for
k=1
,
\beta_{k}^{(t)}=\beta_{1}^{1}=H_{1}^{1}-(\lambda^{*}v^{*})^{-1}\ln\psi(1,-\lambda^{*})\geq-K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*}).

Choose
\Gamma_{0}>2C(\ell^{\prime})+1
. For large
t
, we have

-2K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})>-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t
and hence
\beta_{k}^{(t)}>-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t.

Similarly, when
J<k\leq[V_{t}]
, since
-2+\beta_{J+1}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J+1}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}<0

and
J+1\geq 3
,
, on
I_{2}\cap I_{3}\cap I_{4}
,

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{2}\geq\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{2}\geq-2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]+\beta_{J+1}^{1}+\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J+1}^{2}

\displaystyle>-2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]+\beta_{2}^{1}-2\geq-2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]-\frac{2K_{1}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}-2.

Since
\Gamma_{0}>2C(\ell^{\prime})+1
, we get that for large
t
,
\beta_{k}^{(t)}>-(\Gamma_{0}-1)\ln t.
Note that

\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{(t)}=\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{2}
and by the definition of
I_{5}
,
\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{(t)}\in[-2,-1]
and hence we get (
3.26
).

By (
3.22
) and (
3.26
), we have

\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq\Gamma_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\cap^{5}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)=\Gamma_{3}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{5}\big|\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right).

(3.27)

Since
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
are independent, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{1}\cap I_{2}\right)+\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{3}\cap I_{4}\right)-1

(3.28)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{1}\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{2}\right)+\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{3}\cap I_{4}\right)-1.

(3.29)

By our choice of
J
, conditioned on
\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}
,
we know that

x_{1}:=-2+\beta_{J}^{2}-\beta_{[V_{t}]}^{2}-\beta_{J}^{1}+\beta_{2}^{1}\in\left[0,2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]\right],

(3.30)

and
x_{1}
is independent of
\beta_{2}^{1}
.
Also note that
\beta_{2}^{1}
is independent of
\cap_{j=1}^{4}I_{j}
by Lemma
4.1
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{5}\big|\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{[V_{t}]-2}\inf_{x_{1}\in[0,2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{2}^{1}\in[x_{1},x_{1}+1]\right).

(3.31)

Thus, to continue the estimate in (
3.27
),
we need to estimate
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{3}\cap I_{4}\right)
,
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{2}^{1}\in[x,x+1]\right)
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{j}\right)
for
j=1,2
.
Now we first estimate
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{3}\cap I_{4}\right)
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{2}^{1}\in[x,x+1]\right)
.

Lemma 3.2

(i) For any
\ell^{\prime}>0
,
there exists a non-random constant
C(\ell^{\prime})>0

so that for large
t
,

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{3}\cap I_{4}\right)\geq 1-[V_{t}]^{-\ell^{\prime}}.

(3.32)

(ii) There exists a constant

C_{3}>0

such that for all
x>0
,

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x_{1}\in[0,x]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{2}^{1}\in[x_{1},x_{1}+1]\right)\geq C_{3}e^{-x/C_{3}}.

(3.33)

Proof:

(i) By the definition of
\beta_{k}^{j}
and Lemma
2.3
(2), for

k\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}\right|\leq H_{k}^{j}-H_{k-1}^{j}+\frac{K_{1}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}.

For any
0\leq\eta<\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}=\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}
, by the strong Markov property of
\Xi^{j}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta(H_{k}^{j}-H_{k-1}^{j})\right\}\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta H_{k}^{j}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{k-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{k}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s+\eta H_{k}\right\}\frac{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(k-1,-\lambda^{*})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{K_{1}}\Pi_{k-1}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{es}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})+\eta\right)H_{k}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{-\sqrt{2\left(\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}-\eta\right)}+K_{1}\right\}=:c_{1}<+\infty.

(3.34)

Hence,
for fixed
\eta\in(0,\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es})
,
we have for all
y\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((I_{3}\cap I_{4})^{c}\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\max_{3\leq k\leq[V_{t}]}\max_{j=1,2}\left|\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}\right|>C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=3}^{[V_{t}]}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\left|\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}\right|>C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=3}^{[V_{t}]}\sum_{j=1}^{2}e^{-\eta C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta\left|\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}\right|\right\}\right)\leq 2c_{1}[V_{t}]^{1-\eta C(\ell^{\prime})}\cdot e^{\eta K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})},

Taking
C(\ell^{\prime})
large so that
\eta C(\ell^{\prime})-1>\ell^{\prime}
, we get that (
3.32
) holds for sufficiently large
t
.

(ii) For simplicity, we set
\gamma_{0}:=\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}
. By the definition of
\beta_{2}^{1}
and noting that
0\leq x_{0}:=\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(2,-\lambda^{*})\leq 2K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})
,
for
x_{1}\in[0,x]
,
we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{2}^{1}\in[x_{1},x_{1}+1]\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{2}\in[x_{1}+x_{0},x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{2}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};H_{2}\in[x_{1}+x_{0},x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{y}\left(e^{-\gamma_{0}H_{2}};H_{2}\in[x_{1}+x_{0},x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right)\geq e^{-\gamma_{0}(x_{1}+x_{0}+1)}\Pi_{0}\left(H_{2-y}\in[x_{1}+x_{0},x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right)

(3.35)

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\gamma_{0}(x+2K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})+1)}\Pi_{0}\left(H_{2-y}\in[x_{1}+x_{0}+1/2,x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right).

(3.36)

Since
H_{2-y}
has density
p_{H_{2-y}}(s):=\frac{(2-y)}{\sqrt{2\pi s^{3}}}e^{-(2-y)^{2}/(2s)},
we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(H_{2-y}\in[x_{1}+x_{0}+1/2,x_{1}+x_{0}+1]\right)=\int_{x_{1}+x_{0}+1/2}^{x_{1}+x_{0}+1}\frac{(2-y)}{\sqrt{2\pi s^{3}}}e^{-(2-y)^{2}/(2s)}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\left(x_{1}+1+2K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)^{3}}}e^{-9}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\left(x+1+2K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)^{3}}}e^{-9}.

Since
z<e^{z}
for
z>0
, we have
z^{-3/2}\geq e^{-3z/2}
for
z>0
, and thus there exist

c_{2},c_{3}>0

such that

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x_{1}\in[0,x]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{1}^{1}\in[x_{1},x_{1}+1]\right)\geq c_{2}e^{-c_{3}x},\quad x>0.

Taking
C_{3}=\min\{c_{2},1/c_{3}\}
, we arrive at the desired conclusion.

\Box

The estimates of

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{1}\right)
and
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{2}\right)
are given below.

Lemma 3.3

There exists a non-random
\ell^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for
t
large,

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{1}\right)\geq[V_{t}]^{-\ell^{\prime\prime}};\quad\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{2}\right)\geq[V_{t}]^{-\ell^{\prime\prime}}.

We postpone the proof of Lemma
3.3
to
Subsection
3.4
.

Now we are ready to prove the following estimate on the first moment of
\mathcal{L}_{t}
:

Lemma 3.4

There exists a non-random constant
\ell_{1}>0
such that for
t
large,

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq t^{-\ell_{1}}.

(3.37)

Proof:

Recall the definition of
x_{1}
in (
3.30
).
By (
3.31
) and Lemma
3.2
(ii),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(I_{5}\big|\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{[V_{2}]-2}C_{3}e^{-2C(\ell^{\prime})\ln[V_{t}]/C_{3}}\geq C_{3}[V_{t}]^{-\frac{2C(\ell^{\prime})}{C_{3}}-1}.

(3.38)

By (
3.28
), Lemma
3.2
(i) and Lemma
3.3
, we have

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\cap^{4}_{j=1}I_{j}\right)\geq[V_{t}]^{-2\ell^{\prime\prime}}-[V_{t}]^{-\ell^{\prime}}.

(3.39)

Note that
\ell^{\prime}>0
in Lemma
3.2
is arbitrary.
Letting
\ell^{\prime}>2\ell^{\prime\prime}
and using
(
3.27
), (
3.31
) and (
3.39
),
we finally get that

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{t})\geq\Gamma_{3}C_{3}[V_{t}]^{-2C(\ell^{\prime})/C_{3}-1-2\ell^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\left(1-[V_{t}]^{-(\ell^{\prime}-2\ell^{\prime\prime})}\right).

Combining this with the fact that
V_{t}/t\to v^{*}
, and letting
\ell_{1}>2\ell^{\prime\prime}+2C(\ell^{\prime})/C_{3}+1
, we get the desired result.

\Box

Lemma 3.5

There exists a non-random constant

C_{4}>1

such that for sufficiently large
t
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{P}_{x}^{\xi}\left(\#\left\{\nu\in N(t):X_{t}^{\nu}\in[x-1,x+1]\right\}\leq C_{4}^{t}\right)\leq C_{4}^{-t}.

Proof:
See
[
6
, Lemma 6.8]
for the case of continuous-time branching random walk in random environment or
[
8
, Lemma 4.7]
for the case of branching Brownian motion in random environment.

\Box

Proof of Theorem
1.5
:

Let
\lambda=-\lambda^{*}
and let

\Gamma_{4}
be a constant such that
\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}>\ell_{2}+2\ell_{1}
, where
C_{4}
is the constant in Lemma
3.5
. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define
r_{n}:=[\Gamma_{4}\ln n]
. For simplicity, we will
omit the subscript
n
from
r_{n}
in this proof.
By (
2.27
),
V_{t}-V_{s}\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}{K_{2}}(t-s)

for
t>s.

Let
\Gamma=\lambda^{*}v^{*}\Gamma_{4}/K_{2}+1
, then
V_{n}-V_{n-r}\leq(\Gamma-1)\ln n
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t}-V_{n}<-\Gamma\ln n\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}\left(\#\left\{\nu\in N(r):|X_{r}^{\nu}|\leq 1\right\}\leq C_{4}^{r}\right)+\sup_{|y|\leq 1}\left\{\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t-r}-V_{n}<-\Gamma\ln n\right)\right\}^{C_{4}^{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}n^{-\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}}+\sup_{|y|\leq 1}\left\{\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t-r}-V_{n-r}<-\ln n\right)\right\}^{n^{\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}}/C_{4}}.

(3.40)

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{t}
given by (
3.2
). For large
n
such that
\ln n>3,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t-r}-V_{n-r}\geq-\ln n\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t-r}-V_{n-r}\geq-3\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}(\mathcal{L}_{n-r}>0).

(3.41)

On the other hand, by Lemmas
3.1
and
3.4
, there exists
n_{0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
and
y\in[-1,1]
,

\mathrm{P}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{n-r}>0\right)\geq\frac{\left(\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left(\mathcal{L}_{n-r}\right)\right)^{2}}{\mathrm{E}_{y}^{\xi}\left((\mathcal{L}_{n-r})^{2}\right)}\geq(n-r)^{-\ell_{2}-2\ell_{1}}\geq n^{-\ell_{2}-2\ell_{1}}.

(3.42)

Combining (
3.2
), (
3.2
) and (
3.42
), we get

\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t}-V_{n}<-\Gamma\ln n\right)\leq C_{4}n^{-\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}}+\left(1-n^{-\ell_{2}-2\ell_{1}}\right)^{n^{\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}}/C_{4}}.

Since
1-x\leq e^{-x}
for
x>0
, we have

\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}\left(\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t}-V_{n}<-\Gamma\ln n\right)\leq C_{4}n^{-\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}}+\exp\left\{-n^{\Gamma_{4}\ln C_{4}-\ell_{2}-2\ell_{1}}/C_{4}\right\}.

Applying the Borel-Cantelli lemma, we get

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\inf_{n-1\leq t\leq n}M_{t}-V_{n}}{\ln n}\geq-\Gamma,\quad\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}\mbox{-a.s.}

Since
|V_{n}-V_{t}|
is uniformly bounded for
n
and
t
satisfying
|n-t|\leq 1
, we
get that
\mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}
-almost surely,

\liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{M_{t}-V_{t}}{\ln t}\geq-\Gamma,

which is equivalent to

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{V_{t}-M_{t}}{\ln t}\leq\Gamma.

By Theorem
1.3
,
\lim_{t\to\infty}W_{t}(-\lambda^{*})=0
. Since

W_{t}(-\lambda^{*})\geq\psi(M_{t},-\lambda^{*})e^{-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t},

we have

\lim_{t\to\infty}\psi(M_{t},-\lambda^{*})e^{-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t}=0.

Therefore
\lim_{t\to\infty}(\ln\psi(M_{t},-\lambda^{*}))-\ln\psi(V_{t},-\lambda^{*}))=\lim_{t\to\infty}(-\gamma(\lambda^{*})t+\ln\psi(M_{t},-\lambda^{*}))=-\infty
.
By Remark
2.4
,

M_{t}-V_{t}\to-\infty,\ \mathrm{P}_{0}^{\xi}
-a.s. as
t\to\infty
,
this completes the proof.

\Box

3.3
Proof of Theorem
1.6

In this subsection, we also assume that
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
hold.

Lemma 3.6

Let
\kappa>0
. Suppose that
W(x,\omega):[0,\infty)\times\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
is a continuous process on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})

such that,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely,
W(0,\omega)=0
and

\lim_{x\to\infty}\frac{W(x,\omega)}{x}=0.

Suppose further that the family of processes
\left\{n^{-1}W(nx,\omega)\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges weakly as
n\to\infty
to
\kappa B(x)
, where
B(x)
is a standard Brownian motion on the interval
[0,M]
, for any
M
, in the topology of uniform convergence. Define

h_{t}(\omega)=\sup\left\{h\geq-\lambda^{*}v^{*}t\big|\ W(h+\lambda^{*}v^{*}t)=h-1\right\}.

Then as
t\to\infty
, the family of processes
\left\{\frac{1}{\kappa\sqrt{\lambda^{*}v^{*}n}}h_{nt}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges weakly as
n\to\infty
to a standard Brownian motion on
[0,M]
, in the Skorohod space.

Proof:
See
[
20
, Lemma 3.1]
.

\Box

Lemma 3.7

Let
\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}
be the constant defined in Theorem

1.6
. Under
\mathbb{P}
, we have

\frac{V_{t}-v^{*}t}{\sqrt{t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{d}}}{{\Rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}\right),\,\mbox{ as }t\to\infty.

If
\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}>0
, then the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{V_{nt}-v^{*}nt}{\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}\sqrt{n}},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

converges weakly as
n\to\infty
to a standard Brownian motion on
[0,\infty)
, in the Skorohod topology.

Proof:
By Lemma
2.3
(2) and
(
2.1
),

V_{t}
is the unique solution to

\ln\psi(x,-\lambda^{*})=\lambda^{*}v^{*}t.
By Remark
2.7
, for any fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{V_{t}-v^{*}t}{\sqrt{t}}\leq y\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(V_{t}\leq v^{*}t+y\sqrt{t}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda^{*}v^{*}t\leq\ln\psi(v^{*}t+y\sqrt{t},-\lambda^{*})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(-\ln\phi(v^{*}t+y\sqrt{t},-\lambda^{*})\leq\lambda^{*}y\sqrt{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(-\frac{\ln\phi(v^{*}t+y\sqrt{t},-\lambda^{*})}{\sqrt{v^{*}t+y\sqrt{t}}}\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}}{\sqrt{v^{*}}}y\cdot\sqrt{\frac{t}{t+y\sqrt{t}/v^{*}}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}\left((\sigma_{-\lambda^{*}}^{\prime})\chi\leq\frac{\lambda^{*}}{\sqrt{v^{*}}}y\right)=\mathbb{P}((\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}})\chi\leq y).

Here
\chi
is a standard normal random variable.

If
\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}>0
, let
W(x,\omega):=-\ln\phi\left(\frac{x}{\lambda^{*}},-\lambda^{*},\omega\right)
and
\kappa:=\sigma_{-\lambda^{*}}^{\prime}/\sqrt{\lambda^{*}}>0
.
It is easy to see that

h_{t}(\omega)=\lambda^{*}\left(V_{t+(\lambda^{*}v^{*})^{-1}}(\omega)-v^{*}t\right).

Using Lemma
3.6
, we have
that the sequence of processes

[0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto\frac{h_{nt}}{\kappa\sqrt{\lambda^{*}v^{*}n}}=\frac{V_{nt+(\lambda^{*}v^{*})^{-1}}-v^{*}nt}{\tilde{\sigma}_{-\lambda^{*}}\sqrt{n}},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

converges as
n\to\infty
weakly to a standard Brownian motion on
[0,M]
, for any
M>0
, in the Skorohod topology. According to
[
2
, Lemma 3, p.173]
, this is equivalent to the weak convergence to a standard Brownian motion on
[0,\infty)
in the Skorohod topology.
By Remark
2.4
,
\big|V_{t+(\lambda^{*}v^{*})^{-1}}-V_{t}\big|\leq 1/K_{2}
all
t\geq 0
, hence the second conclusion of the lemma is valid.

\Box

Proof of Theorem
1.6
:
This follows from Theorem
1.5
and Lemma
3.7
.

\Box

3.4
Proof of Lemma
3.3

In this subsection we give the proof of Lemma
3.3
. We first prove several lemmas.

Lemma 3.8

There exists a non-random constant

C_{5}>0

such that for
j=1,2
,

\inf_{y\in[-1,1],k\in\mathbb{N},k\geq 2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}>C_{5}\right)>C_{5},\qquad\inf_{y\in[-1,1],k\in\mathbb{N},k\geq 2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{j}-\beta_{k-1}^{j}<-C_{5}\right)>C_{5}.

Proof:

Since
\beta^{1}
and
\beta^{2}
are identically distributed, we only prove the case
j=2
.
Recall that
\beta_{k}^{2}=H_{k}^{2}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})
and
0<K_{2}\leq\ln\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})-\ln\psi(k-1,-\lambda^{*})\leq K_{1}
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}

and
k\geq 2
.
Note that for any Borel set
B\subset[0,+\infty)
with
m(B)>0
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{2}-H_{k-1}^{2}\in B\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{2}\in B\right)\geq\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}\in B\right).

For
0<\delta<K_{2}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{k-1}^{2}<-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{2}-H_{k-1}^{2}<-\delta+\frac{K_{2}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}<-\delta+K_{2}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right).

Similarly,

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{k-1}^{2}>\delta\right)\geq\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}>\delta+K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right).

When
\delta=0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}<K_{2}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)>0=\delta,

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}>K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)>0=\delta.

Since
H_{1}
has a density,
the maps
\delta\mapsto\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}<-\delta+K_{2}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)
and

\delta\mapsto\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}>\delta+K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)

are continuous in
\delta>0
. Thus there exists
C_{5}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}<-C_{5}+K_{2}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)>C_{5},

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}\left(e^{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right)};H_{1}>C_{5}+K_{1}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\right)>C_{5}.

This completes the proof.

\Box

Lemma 3.9

For any
\kappa_{0}>0
,
there exist non-random constants

N(\kappa_{0})>0
and

C_{6}=C_{6}(\kappa_{0})\in(0,1)

such that for all

r\geq 2,k\geq N(\kappa_{0})
and
j=1,2,

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r<m\leq r+k}\left(H_{m}^{j}-H_{r}^{j}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{m}^{j}-H_{r}^{j}\right)\right)\geq-\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}\right)\geq C_{6}.

Proof:

We only deal with
j=1
, the case
j=2
is similar. Define

\Delta H_{k}:=H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}\right)

(3.43)

and

f(\eta):=\ln\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1})\right\}\right).

Note that by (
2.2
),
(
2.15
)
and (
2.10
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}\right)=\Pi_{y}\left(H_{k}\exp\left\{-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})H_{k}+\int_{0}^{H_{k}}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\}\frac{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*})}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}\cdot\frac{1}{-\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda}\Pi_{y}\left(\exp\left\{-\gamma(\lambda)H_{k}+\int_{0}^{H_{k}}(m-1)\xi(B_{s})\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)\bigg|_{\lambda=-\lambda^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}\cdot\frac{1}{-\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda}\left(\frac{\psi(y,\lambda,\omega)}{\psi(k,\lambda,\omega)}\right)\bigg|_{\lambda=-\lambda^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\psi(k-y,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{y}\omega)}{-\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda}\left(\frac{1}{\psi(k-y,\lambda,\theta_{y}\omega)}\right)\bigg|_{\lambda=-\lambda^{*}}=\frac{1}{\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k-y,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{y}\omega)}{\psi(k-y,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{y}\omega)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\left[\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(y,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(y,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}\right].

Thus

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}\right)-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k-1}^{1}\right)=\frac{1}{\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k-1,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(k-1,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}\right).

(3.44)

Using Lemma
2.3
(1), we obtain

\left|\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}\right)-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k-1}^{1}\right)\right|\leq\frac{C_{1}(-\lambda^{*})}{\left|\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\right|},

(3.45)

here
C_{1}(-\lambda^{*})
is the constant in Lemma
2.3
.

By (
3.2
), we have, uniformly for any
0\leq\eta\leq\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}\right)/2

and
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(\eta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1})\right\}(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1})\right)}{\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1})\right\}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta H_{k}^{1}\right\}H_{k}^{1}\right)}{\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta H_{k}^{1}\right\}\right)}\leq\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta H_{k}^{1}\right\}H_{k}^{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}-\eta\right)\right\}H_{1}\right)\cdot e^{K_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{-H_{1}\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}\right)/2\right\}H_{1}\right)\cdot e^{K_{1}}=:c_{1}<+\infty.

This implies that
f(\eta)\leq\eta c_{1}
for any
0\leq\eta\leq\left(\gamma(\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}\right)/2
.
Therefore, by (
3.45
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\eta|\Delta H_{k}|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1})\right\}\right)\cdot\exp\left\{\eta\left|\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}\right)\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{\eta c_{1}+\eta\left|C_{1}(-\lambda^{*})/\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\right|\right\}.

Thus,
\Delta H_{k}
are sub-exponential random variables for
k\geq 1
. By
[
24
, Proposition 2.7.1]
,
there exists

c_{2}

depending only on

c_{1}+C_{1}(-\lambda^{*})/\left|\gamma^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\right|

such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\eta\Delta H_{k}}\right)\leq\exp\left\{(c_{2}\eta)^{2}\right\},\quad|\eta|\leq 1/c_{2},k\geq 1.

(3.46)

By martingale theory and Doob’s inequality, for
0<\eta<1/(2c_{2})
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r<m\leq r+k}\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{m}\Delta H_{\ell}\geq-\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}\right)=1-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\sup_{r<m\leq r+k}\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{m}(-\Delta H_{\ell})>\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\sup_{r<m\leq r+k}\exp\left\{\eta\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{m}(-\Delta H_{\ell})\right\}>e^{\eta\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-2\eta\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{2\eta\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{r+k}(-\Delta H_{\ell})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=1-e^{-2\eta\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}}\cdot\prod_{\ell=r+1}^{r+k}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{2\eta(-\Delta H_{\ell})\right\}\right)\geq 1-\exp\left\{4(c_{2}\eta)^{2}k-2\eta\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}\right\}.

Taking
\eta:=\kappa_{0}/(4c_{2}^{2}\sqrt{k})\leq 1/(2c_{2})

and
N(\kappa_{0}):=(\kappa_{0}/(2c_{2}))^{2}
, then for
k\geq N(\kappa_{0})
,

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r<m\leq r+k}\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{m}\Delta H_{\ell}\geq-\kappa_{0}\sqrt{k}\right)\geq C_{6}(\kappa_{0}):=1-\exp\left\{-\kappa_{0}^{2}/(4c_{2}^{2})\right\}\in(0,1).

The proof is now complete.

\Box

Lemma 3.10

Let
\Delta H_{k}
be defined by (
3.43
). There exist
positive constants
\alpha^{*}
and

C_{7}
, independent of
k
and
r
,
such that for all
0\leq x\leq\alpha^{*}k
and
k,r\in\mathbb{Z}^{+},

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\sum_{s=r+1}^{r+k}\Delta H_{s}\geq x\right)\geq C_{7}\exp\left\{-\frac{x^{2}}{C_{7}k}\right\}.

Proof:

We continue the constant label in the proof of Lemma
3.9
.
We claim that

c_{3}:=\textup{essinf}_{\xi,k}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((\Delta H_{k})^{2}\right)>0.

(3.47)

In fact, for
x\in(0,1)
, let

\Delta_{x}H_{k}:=H_{k-1+x}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k-1+x}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}\right).

By the strong Markov property, under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
,
\Delta_{x}H_{k}
and
\Delta H_{k}-\Delta_{x}H_{k}
are independent. Since
\Delta H_{k}
and
\Delta_{x}H_{k}
are both centered, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((\Delta H_{k})^{2}\right)>\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((\Delta_{x}H_{k})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle={\Pi}_{k-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{k-1+x}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\frac{\psi(k-1+x,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(k-1,-\lambda^{*})}{(H_{k-1+x})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k-1+x}^{1}-H_{k-1}^{1}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{0}\left({(H_{x})}^{2}\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}\right){H_{x}}\right\}\right)-e^{2K_{1}x}\left(\Pi_{0}\left({H_{x}}\exp\left\{-\left(\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}\right){H_{x}}\right\}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=x^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2u_{2}}e^{-x\sqrt{2u_{2}}}-\frac{1}{2u_{1}}e^{-2x(\sqrt{2u_{1}}-K_{1})}\right)+\frac{x}{2\sqrt{2u_{2}^{3}}}e^{-x\sqrt{2u_{2}}}=:\widehat{F}(x),

where
u_{1}:=\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}\leq\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{ei}=:u_{2},
and in the last equality we used (
2.21
). Since
\lim_{x\to 0}\frac{\widehat{F}(x)}{x}>0
, there exists
x
such that for
all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and all realizations of
\xi
,

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((\Delta H_{k})^{2}\right)>\widehat{F}(x)>0
,
which implies the claim holds.

By the sub-exponential property of
\Delta H_{k}
given by (
3.46
),
there exists a constant
K>0
,
independent of
k
, such that
for all
\ell\geq 1
,
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\left|\Delta H_{k}\right|^{\ell}\right)\leq(K\ell)^{\ell}

(see
[
24
, Proposition 2.7.1, (ii)]
). Therefore, by the trivial inequality that
\ell!\geq(\ell/e)^{\ell},
when
|\eta|<1/(2eK),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\eta\Delta H_{k}}\right)=1+\frac{1}{2!}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left((\Delta H_{k})^{2}\right)\eta^{2}+\sum_{\ell=3}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\ell!}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\left(\Delta H_{k}\right)^{\ell}\right)|\eta|^{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq 1+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\eta^{2}-\sum_{\ell=3}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\ell/e)^{\ell}}(K\ell|\eta|)^{\ell}=1+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\eta^{2}-\sum_{\ell=3}^{\infty}(eK|\eta|)^{\ell}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\eta^{2}-\frac{(eK)^{3}|\eta|^{3}}{1-eK|\eta|}\geq 1+\left(\frac{c_{3}}{2}-\frac{(eK)^{3}|\eta|}{2}\right)\eta^{2},

where
c_{3}
is given by (
3.47
).
Now we choose

c_{4}>0
and
c_{5}

such that

(eK)^{3}c_{4}=c_{3}/2

and that
1+c_{3}c_{4}^{2}/4=e^{c_{5}c_{4}^{2}}
, then for all
|\eta|{\leq}c_{4}
, we have

1+\left(\frac{c_{3}}{2}-\frac{(eK)^{3}|\eta|}{2}\right)\eta^{2}\geq 1+\frac{c_{3}}{4}\eta^{2}\geq e^{c_{5}\eta^{2}},

here in the last inequality we used the fact that the function
h(x):=e^{c_{5}x}-1-c_{3}x/4
is non-positive for
x\in(0,c_{4}^{2})
, which follows easily from our choice of
c_{5}
and
c_{4}
.
Thus we have

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\eta\Delta H_{k}}\right)\geq e^{c_{5}\eta^{2}},\quad{|\eta|\leq c_{4},k\geq 1}.

(3.48)

Now, using (
3.46
) and (
3.48
),
the desired conclusion follows from
[
26
, Theorem 4]
by taking

M={c_{4}\wedge(1/2c_{2})},\alpha=1,C_{1}=(c_{2})^{2},c_{1}=C_{5},c^{\prime\prime}={M}^{2},u_{1}=u_{2}=\cdots=u_{k}=1
and
\alpha^{*}=c^{\prime}M\alpha
with
c^{\prime}>0
being the constant in
[
26
, Theorem 4]
,
and
c_{2}
is the constant in (
3.46
).

\Box

For
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\rho_{\ell}:=\frac{\ln\psi(\ell,-\lambda^{*})-\ln\psi(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}+\frac{1}{v^{*}}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}\right).

By (
2.29
),

\rho_{\ell}=\frac{\ln\phi(\ell,-\lambda^{*})-\ln\phi(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}+\frac{1}{v^{*}}\left(\frac{\phi_{\lambda}(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}{\phi(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\phi_{\lambda}(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\phi(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}\right).

(3.49)

Taking logarithm and differentiating with respect to
\lambda
in (
2.10
), and
letting
\lambda=-\lambda^{*}
, we get

\displaystyle\lambda^{*}v^{*}\rho_{\ell}=

\displaystyle-\ln\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)

\displaystyle-\lambda^{*}v^{*}\frac{\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(H_{\ell}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)}{\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)}.

Define

\displaystyle A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}>j\right),

\displaystyle A_{2}(-\lambda^{*})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}\leq j\right),

\displaystyle A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(H_{\ell}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}>j\right),

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(H_{\ell}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}\leq j\right).

Then

\rho_{\ell}=-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\left(A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})+A_{2}(-\lambda^{*})\right)-\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})+A_{2}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}{A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})+A_{2}(-\lambda^{*})}.

Note that

A_{1}(-\lambda^{*}),A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\cap\mathcal{F}^{j}
.
Using the same notation in (
2.31
),

\displaystyle A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})\leq\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{es}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)H_{\ell}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{es}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)H_{1}\right\}\right)=c_{1}(-\lambda^{*})<1,

and when
\ell\geq j+2,
we have
j-(\ell-1)\leq-1
and thus

\displaystyle A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})\geq\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{ei}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)H_{\ell}\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}>j\right)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{ei}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)H_{1}\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{1}}B_{s}>j-(\ell-1)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{ei}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)H_{1}\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{1}}B_{s}>-1\right)=c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})>0.

If
H_{\ell}\geq\ell-j
, then by
(H2)
,

\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\leq\exp\left\{\left((m-1)\textup{es}-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)(\ell-j)\right\};

and if
H_{\ell}\leq\ell-j
, then

\displaystyle\left\{\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}\leq j\right\}\subset\left\{\inf_{0\leq s\leq\ell-j}B_{s}\leq j\right\}.

Letting

\gamma_{0}:=\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}>0
, when
\ell\geq j+2
, by the reflection principle and Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle A_{2}(-\lambda^{*})\leq e^{-\gamma_{0}(\ell-j)}+\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\inf_{0\leq s\leq\ell-j}B_{s}\leq j\right)=e^{-\gamma_{0}(\ell-j)}+\Pi_{0}\left(\inf_{0\leq s\leq\ell-j}B_{s}\leq j-(\ell-1)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\gamma_{0}(\ell-)}+2\Pi_{0}\left(B_{\ell-j}\leq j-\ell+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\gamma_{0}(\ell-j)}+2\frac{\Pi_{0}(e^{-B_{\ell-j}/2})}{e^{-(j-\ell+1)/2}}=e^{-\gamma_{0}(\ell-j)}+2\frac{e^{(\ell-j)/8}}{e^{-(j-\ell+1)/2}}\leq(1+2e^{1/2})e^{-(\ell-j)\delta}

with
\delta:=\min\{3/8,\gamma_{0}\}.

Now we take a constant
\lambda_{0}
such that
-\rho>\lambda_{0}>-\lambda^{*}
and
\gamma(-\lambda^{*})>\gamma(\lambda_{0})>(m-1)\textup{es}
. Then
there is a constant

c>0

such that
\sup_{x>0}\left\{xe^{-(\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-\gamma(\lambda_{0}))x}\right\}\leq c
,
and then

\displaystyle 0\leq A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\leq c\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(\lambda_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}>j\right)=c{A}_{1}(\lambda_{0}),

\displaystyle 0\leq A_{2}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})\leq c\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(\lambda_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\};\inf_{0\leq s\leq H_{\ell}}B_{s}\leq j\right)=c{A}_{2}(\lambda_{0}).

Noticing that
\gamma(\lambda_{0})>(m-1)\textup{es}
,
using argument similar to those used for
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, with
-\lambda^{*}
replaced by
\lambda_{0}
, we have that there
exist positive constants
\tilde{\delta},c_{3}(\lambda_{0}),c_{4}
such that

{A}_{1}(\lambda_{0})<c_{3}(\lambda_{0}),\quad{A}_{2}(\lambda_{0})\leq c_{4}e^{-(\ell-j)\tilde{\delta}},\quad\forall\ell\geq j+2.

Therefore, setting
A_{i}=A_{i}(-\lambda^{*}),A_{i}^{\prime}=A_{i}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})
for simplicity, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1})+\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}}{A_{1}}\right|=\left|\frac{-\ln(A_{1}+A_{2})-\lambda^{*}v^{*}\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}+A_{2}^{\prime}}{A_{1}+A_{2}}}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1})+\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}}{A_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\left|\ln(1+A_{2}/A_{1})\right|+\frac{A_{1}A_{2}^{\prime}+A_{2}A_{1}^{\prime}}{(A_{1}+A_{2})A_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\frac{A_{2}}{A_{1}}+\frac{c_{1}(-\lambda^{*})cc_{4}e^{-(\ell-j)\tilde{\delta}}+(1+2e^{1/2})e^{-(\ell-j)\delta}c{c}_{3}(\lambda_{0})}{c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\frac{(1+2e^{1/2})e^{-(\ell-j)\delta}}{c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})}+\frac{c_{1}(-\lambda^{*})cc_{4}e^{-(\ell-j)\tilde{\delta}}+(1+2e^{1/2})e^{-(\ell-j)\delta}cc_{3}(\lambda_{0})}{c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})^{2}}.

Thus there exists some constant
C_{8}>0
such that for

\forall\ell\geq j+2
,

\left|\rho_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1})+\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}}{A_{1}}\right|\leq C_{8}e^{-(\ell-j)\delta^{\prime}}

(3.50)

with
\delta^{\prime}:=\min\{\delta,\tilde{\delta}\}>0
.
Define

\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}:=-\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1}(-\lambda^{*}))-\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}{A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})}+C_{8}e^{-(\ell-j)\delta^{\prime}}.

Then by (
3.50
), for
\ell\geq j+2
, we have that

\displaystyle 0\leq\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}-\rho_{\ell}=

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1}(-\lambda^{*}))+\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}{A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})}\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\rho}_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln(A_{1}(-\lambda^{*}))+\frac{A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})}{A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{8}e^{-(\ell-j)\delta^{\prime}}.

(3.51)

Also,
|\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}|
is uniformly bounded for all
\ell\geq j+2
:

\|\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}v^{*}}\ln\frac{1}{c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})}+\frac{cc_{3}(\lambda_{0})}{c_{2}(-\lambda^{*})}+C_{8}=:M_{\rho}.

By the definitions of
A_{1}(-\lambda^{*})
and
A_{1}^{\prime}(-\lambda^{*})
, we know that

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\cap\mathcal{F}^{j}\quad\mbox{when}\quad\ell\geq j+2.

(3.52)

For
q\geq 0
and
i\geq 1
, let
t_{i}:=2^{i}
and define

\displaystyle r_{0}

\displaystyle:=t_{i-1},\quad L_{i}:=\left\lceil\frac{C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}}{16M_{\rho}}\right\rceil,

\displaystyle s_{0}

\displaystyle:=t_{i}\land\inf\left\{k\geq r_{0}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{0}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{0}-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\},\quad r_{q+1}:=s_{q}+L_{i},

\displaystyle s_{q+1}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{k\geq r_{q+1}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{q+1}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{q+1}-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}\land\left(r_{q+1}+t_{i}-t_{i-1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{k:s_{k}\geq t_{i}\},

(3.53)

here
C_{5}
is the constant defined in Lemma
3.8
.

For
\ell<t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, define

D(\ell):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{r=1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{r}^{(-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\forall k<\ell,\quad\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\overline{\rho}_{r}^{(-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}.

Also define

D(t_{i}-t_{i-1}):=\left\{\omega:\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\overline{\rho}_{r}^{(-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\,\forall\ell<t_{i}-t_{i-1}\right\}.

Proposition 3.11

Fix
i\geq 1
.

(i) For any
k\geq 1
and positive integers
\ell_{0},...,\ell_{k-1}<t_{i}-t_{i-1},\ell_{k}\leq t_{i}-t_{i-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right)\leq\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}\prod_{j=0}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell_{j})\right).

(3.54)

(ii) For any
k\geq 1
and
\ell_{0},...,\ell_{k}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, when
i
is sufficiently large so that
t_{i-1}>L_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left\{\mathfrak{K}=k,s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right\}\in\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}}.

(3.55)

Proof:

(i)
By induction, we only need to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1,s_{k}-r_{k}=\ell_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right)\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell_{k})\right)\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right).

(3.56)

For integers
r,s
such that
r<s
and
s-r<t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, define

\displaystyle D(r,s):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\,\forall k\in[r+1,s),\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{s}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}.

(3.57)

Also, when
s-r=t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, we define

\displaystyle D(r,s):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\,\forall k\in[r+1,r+t_{i}-t_{i-1})\right\}.

(3.58)

Then if
\ell_{j}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}
for all
0\leq j\leq k
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left\{s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D(t_{i-1},t_{i-1}+\ell_{0})\cap D(t_{i-1}+\ell_{0}+L_{i},t_{i-1}+\ell_{0}+L_{i}+\ell_{1})

\displaystyle\cap\cdots\cap D(t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1},t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k}).

(3.59)

It follows from (
3.52
) that
D(r,s)\in\mathcal{F}_{s}\cap\mathcal{F}^{r-[L_{i}/2]}
when
s-r\leq t_{i}-t_{i-1}
. Using (
3.4
), we get

\displaystyle\left\{s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right\}\in\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}+(k-1)L_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1}},

\displaystyle\{s_{k}-r_{k}=\ell_{k}\}\in\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1}-[L_{i}/2]}.

(3.60)

By the definition of
L_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left(t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1}-[L_{i}/2]\right)-\left(t_{i-1}+(k-1)L_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle=L_{i}-[L_{i}/2]\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho}),

together with the
\zeta
-mixing condition we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right)

(3.61)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\big)

(3.62)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{P}\big(D(t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k-1},t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k})\big)\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right)

(3.63)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right)\mathbb{P}(D(\ell_{k}))\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right),

(3.64)

where in the last equality, we used the property that
\mathbb{P}(D(r,s))=\mathbb{P}(D(0,s-r))=\mathbb{P}(D(s-r))
.
Thus (
3.4
) is valid.

(ii)
Note that

\displaystyle\left\{\mathfrak{K}=k,s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right\}=\left\{s_{k}\geq t_{i},s_{k-1}<t_{i},s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k,s_{k-1}=t_{i-1}+(k-1)L_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ell_{j}<t_{i},s_{k}=t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}\ell_{j}\geq t_{i}\right\}.

The event in (
3.55
) is not empty only if
t_{i-1}+(k-1)L_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\ell_{j}<t_{i}
and
t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}\ell_{j}\geq t_{i}
. Combining this observation with (
3.4
), we get that, when
t_{i-1}>L_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left\{\mathfrak{K}=k,s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k\right\}\in\left(\bigcup_{j=0}^{k}\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}+jL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{j-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\right)\bigcap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i-1}+kL_{i}+\ell_{0}+...+\ell_{k}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\cap\mathcal{F}_{L_{i}+\ell_{k}+t_{i}}\subset\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\cap\mathcal{F}_{L_{i}+(t_{i}-t_{i-1})+t_{i}}\subset\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}}.

The proof is now complete.

\Box

Put

\alpha_{1}:=2\alpha^{*}/(3C_{5}),\quad\alpha_{2}:=\alpha^{*}/(6C_{5}),

(3.65)

where
\alpha^{*}
is the constant in Lemma
3.10
,
and
C_{5}
the constant in Lemma
3.8
.
Define

G_{i}^{1}:=\left\{\omega:\,\max_{r\in[t_{i-1},t_{i+1}],0<k\leq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\ \sum_{\ell=r+1}^{r+k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(t_{i-2})}\leq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/32\right\}.

Lemma 3.12

(i) There exists a constant

C_{9}>0

such that for sufficiently large
i
,
on
G_{i}^{1}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x\geq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4},k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=x\right)

\displaystyle\geq C_{9}^{\mathfrak{K}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}=:Z_{i}^{1},

(3.66)

(ii) There exist constants
\varepsilon>0
and

K=K(\epsilon)

such that for
i
large enough,

\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\right\}\right)\leq K.

(3.67)

Proof:

(i)

Step 1

Recall the definition of
\beta^{1}_{k}
in (
3.18
).
For
j\geq 0
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right\}\bigcap\bigcap_{k=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\left\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{16}C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{k=s_{j}+1}^{r_{j+1}}\left\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{16}C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right\},\quad\mathcal{B}:=\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}}\mathcal{A}_{j}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}\right).

Put

\displaystyle\left(Z_{i}^{1}\right)^{*}:=

\displaystyle\inf_{x\geq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4},k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4},k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right),

here the last equality follows from the following argument: since
\{\beta_{k}^{1},k\geq 1\}
has independent increments,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4},k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=x\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2-x,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}-x,k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=x\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2-x,\ \beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}-x,k=t_{i-1}+1,...,t_{i}\right)

and the last quantity is decreasing in
x
.

In this step we will show that

\left(Z_{i}^{1}\right)^{*}\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{B}\,\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right).

(3.68)

Given that
\mathcal{B}
occurs, under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}(\ \cdot\ |\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2)
, we have, for
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}
and
r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}
,

\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\quad\mbox{ on }\mathcal{A}_{j}.

By the definition of
s_{j}
we have that
for all
r_{j}<k<s_{j}
,

\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{j}-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,

and for
k=s_{j}
,

\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\rho_{\ell}\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}-1}\rho_{\ell}+M_{\rho}\leq M_{\rho}+C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16.

Thus, for
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}
and
r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}
, we have

\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}.

(3.69)

Note that

\beta_{r_{0}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2=C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(2\sqrt{2}).

(3.70)

Also for
\mathfrak{K}-1\geq j\geq 0
,
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}
we have

\beta_{r_{j+1}}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}\geq-\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{r_{j+1}}\rho_{\ell}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\geq-\left\lceil\frac{C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}}{16M_{\rho}}\right\rceil M_{\rho}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}

(3.71)

and on
\mathcal{A}_{j}
,
\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}
. Hence, together with (
3.71
), we have
