\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=(\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j-1}}^{1})+(\beta_{s_{j-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1})\geq 7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho},\quad\mathfrak{K}\geq j\geq 1.

(3.72)

So, combining (
3.69
), (
3.70
) and (
3.72
), we have, for
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}
and
r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}
,

\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq\min\left\{C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(2\sqrt{2}),7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}\right\}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}.

Let
i
be sufficiently large so that

\min\left\{C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(2\sqrt{2}),7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}\right\}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}>t_{i}^{1/4}.

Then
\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}
for all
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}
and
r_{j}\leq k\leq s_{j}
when
i
is sufficiently large.
Similarly, when
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}-1
and
s_{j}<k<r_{j+1}
, on
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}
,

\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}\geq-\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\geq-\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{r_{j+1}}M_{\rho}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho},

which implies
\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}
since on
\mathcal{A}_{j}
,
\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}
. For large
i
such that
7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}>t_{i}^{1/4}
, we also have
\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}
holds for all
0\leq j\leq\mathfrak{K}-1
and
s_{j}<k<r_{j+1}
.
We have also proved that, for all
s_{0}\leq k\leq s_{\mathfrak{K}}
,

\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}.

Finally, to show
\beta_{t_{i}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2
holds for large
i
, it suffices to prove that
s_{0}\leq t_{i}\leq s_{\mathfrak{K}}
, which is trivial by the definitions of
s_{j}
and
\mathfrak{K}
,.
Thus (
3.68
) is valid.

Step 2
In this step we will show that
there exists a constant

C_{9}>0
such that

\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{B}\,\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right)\geq C_{9}^{\mathfrak{K}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}.

(3.73)

By the strong Markov property,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{B}\Big|\beta_{t_{i-1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0}\Big|\beta_{r_{0}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right)\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\,\Big|\beta_{s_{0}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{1}\,\Big|\beta_{r_{1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}\right)\times...\times\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{K}}\,\Big|\beta_{r_{\mathfrak{K}}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{0}\Big|\beta_{r_{0}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2\right)\prod_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}-1}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j})\prod_{j=1}^{\mathfrak{K}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}\Big|\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}\right).

(3.74)

Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}
can be rewritten as

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}:=\left\{\inf_{s_{j}<k\leq r_{j+1}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{s_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{s_{j}}^{1}\right)\right)\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}.

This is because

\displaystyle\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}=\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\left(\beta_{\ell}^{1}-\beta_{\ell-1}^{1}+\rho_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}+\frac{1}{v^{*}}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(\ell,-\lambda^{*})}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}{\psi(\ell-1,-\lambda^{*})}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}-\frac{\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(H_{\ell}\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)}{\Pi_{\ell-1}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{\ell}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s})-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{\ell-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}\right)\right)=\sum_{\ell=s_{j}+1}^{k}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}\right)\right).

Also note that
r_{j+1}-s_{j}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(16M_{\rho})+1<t_{i}
for sufficiently large
i
and
r_{j+1}-s_{j}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(16M_{\rho})
.
Taking
\kappa_{0}=C_{5}/16

in Lemma
3.9
,

C_{6}:=C_{6}(\kappa_{0})\in(0,1)
with
\kappa_{0}=C_{5}/16

such that for large
i
,

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{j}\right)\geq C_{6}.

(3.75)

Define
d_{0}=C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2
and
d_{j}=7C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/8-M_{\rho}
for
j\geq 1
. Let
f(X)=x_{1}+...+x_{s_{j}-r_{j}},g(X)=\inf_{1\leq k\leq s_{j}-r_{j}}(x_{1}+...+x_{k})
and
X=(x_{1},...,x_{s_{j}-r_{j}}).
By Harris’ inequality, see
[
5
, Theorem 2.15]
, we have
for all
j\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}\Big|\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=d_{j}\right)\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\Big|\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=d_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r_{j}<k\leq s_{j}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}\right)\right)\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}-d_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r_{j}<k\leq s_{j}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}\right)\right)\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r_{j}<k\leq s_{j}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}\right)\right)\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right)

\displaystyle=:(I)\times(II).

(3.76)

We treat
(I)
first.
Note that
C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16+M_{\rho}\leq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2
for large
i
. By the definition of
s_{j}
, we have

\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\rho_{\ell}\leq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2
and

\displaystyle(I)=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{s_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\rho_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\rho_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{s_{j}}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{s_{j}}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}\right)\geq 3C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2\right).

(3.77)

By the definitions of
s_{j}
and
r_{j}
, we have
t_{i}\geq s_{j}-r_{j}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(16M_{\rho})
.
By (
3.4
) and the definition of
s_{j}
, for large
i
such that

2C_{8}t_{i}e^{-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\delta^{\prime}/(32M_{\rho})}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32
,
when
s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}-t_{i-1},

\displaystyle C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{j}-[L_{i}/2])}\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}{\rho}_{\ell}+2C_{8}(s_{j}-r_{j})\exp\left\{-\left(r_{j}+1-\left(r_{j}-[L_{i}/2]\right)\right)\delta^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}{\rho}_{\ell}+2C_{8}t_{i}e^{-\left([L_{i}/2]+1\right)\delta^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}{\rho}_{\ell}+2C_{8}t_{i}e^{-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\delta^{\prime}/(32M_{\rho})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}{\rho}_{\ell}+C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\leq\sum_{\ell=r_{j}+1}^{s_{j}}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(t_{i-2})}+C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32.

This implies that,
on
G_{i}^{1}
,

s_{j}-r_{j}\geq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}
.
When
s_{j}-r_{j}=t_{i}-t_{i-1}
,
we can also take
i
large so that

s_{j}-r_{j}=t_{i}-t_{i-1}>t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}
.
By the definitions of
\alpha_{1}
(see (
3.65
)) and
\alpha^{*}
, we have
\alpha^{*}(s_{j}-r_{j})\geq 3C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2
and hence by
Lemma
3.10
(with
k=s_{j}-r_{j}
and
x=3C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/2
) and (
3.4
),
the constant
C_{7}
defined in Lemma
3.10
satisfies that on
G_{i}^{1}
and for any
j
,

(I)\geq C_{7}\exp\left\{-\frac{9C_{5}^{2}t_{i}}{4C_{7}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}.

(3.78)

Now we treat
(II)
.
Taking
\kappa_{0}=C_{5}/16
in Lemma
3.9
, we get

(II)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\inf_{r_{j}<k\leq s_{j}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(H_{k}^{1}-H_{r_{j}}^{1}\right)\right)\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right)\geq C_{6},

(3.79)

where
C_{6}=C_{6}(\kappa_{0})
with
\kappa_{0}=C_{5}/16
.
If
s_{j}=r_{j}+t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, then
s_{j}-r_{j}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(16M_{\rho})
for large
i
and (
3.79
) also holds.
Plugging (
3.78
) and (
3.79
) into (
3.4
), we obtain

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}\Big|\beta_{r_{j}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=d_{j}\right)\geq C_{7}\cdot C_{6}\exp\left\{-\frac{9C_{5}^{2}t_{i}}{4C_{7}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}.

(3.80)

Combining (
3.4
), (
3.75
) and (
3.80
), we conclude that
on
G_{i}^{1}
,

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{1}

\displaystyle\geq C_{7}\cdot C_{6}(C_{7}\cdot(C_{6})^{2})^{\mathfrak{K}}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}}\frac{9C_{5}^{2}t_{i}}{4C_{7}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq(C_{7}\cdot(C_{6})^{2})^{\mathfrak{K}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}}\frac{9C_{5}^{2}t_{i}}{4C_{7}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}.

Since
\mathfrak{K}
and
s_{j}-r_{j}
does not depend on
y\in[-1,1]
, (
3.73
) holds with
C_{9}:=\left(C_{7}\cdot C_{6}^{2}\right)\wedge\left(4C_{7}/(9C_{5}^{2})\right)
.

Combining Step 1 and Step 2, we get (
3.12
).

(ii)
Define

\mathfrak{J}:=\inf\{j:s_{j}-r_{j}\geq t_{i}-t_{i-1}\}.

Then
\mathfrak{K}\leq\mathfrak{J}
. In fact, on the event
\mathfrak{K}=k
, we have
s_{k}\geq t_{i}
but
s_{k-1}<t_{i}
, and then for all
0\leq j<k,

s_{j}-r_{j}\leq s_{k-1}-t_{i-1}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}
, which implies that
\mathfrak{J}\geq k
.
For
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
, by (
3.12
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\right\}\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{1}}\left(-\left(\mathfrak{J}+1\right)\ln C_{9}+\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{J}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\left(-\left(\mathfrak{J}+1\right)\ln C_{9}+\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{J}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{C_{9}}\right)^{\varepsilon(k+1)}\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{k}-r_{k})}+\varepsilon\sum_{j=0}^{{k-1}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}1_{\{\mathfrak{J}=k\}}\right).

(3.81)

By (
3.54
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{k}-r_{k})}+\varepsilon\sum_{j=0}^{{k-1}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}1_{\{\mathfrak{J}=k\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{k}-r_{k})}\right\}1_{\{s_{k}-r_{k}=t_{i}-t_{i-1}\}}\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(s_{j}-r_{j})}\right\}1_{\{s_{j}-r_{j}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}\}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{0},...,\ell_{k-1}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell_{j}}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}-r_{j}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}\sum_{1\leq\ell_{0},...,\ell_{k-1}<t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell_{j}}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell_{j})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell)\right)\right)^{k}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon(k+1)}}\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell)\right)\right)^{k}.

(3.82)

Note that
t_{i}-t_{i-1}=t_{i}/2
, and
D(\ell)
are disjoint for all
\ell<t_{i}/2
.
Therefore, for all
q<t_{i}/2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbb{P}(D(\ell))=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{q}D(\ell)\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right).

(3.83)

Using (
3.4
), for any
q<t_{i}/2
and large
i
such that
C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16-2C_{8}e^{-([L_{i}/2]+1)\delta^{\prime}}\cdot t_{i}/2\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16-2C_{8}e^{-(1+[L_{i}/2])\delta^{\prime}}\cdot q\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right).

(3.84)

Recall that
\rho_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}
and that
\mathbb{E}(\rho_{\ell})=0
, so for all
k\leq\ell
, by
\zeta
-mixing, we have that

\left|\mathbb{E}\left(\rho_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{\ell}\right)\right|\leq\mathbb{E}(|\rho_{k}|)\zeta(\ell-k)
and therefore, for all
\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\left(\left\|\rho_{j}^{2}\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|\rho_{\ell}\sum_{k=j}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\rho_{k}|\mathcal{F}^{\ell}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq M_{\rho}^{2}+\sup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}2\|\rho_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\sum_{k=j}^{\ell-1}\mathbb{E}(|\rho_{k}|)\zeta(\ell-k)\leq M_{\rho}^{2}+2M_{\rho}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\zeta(k)<\infty.

By
[
22
, Theorem 2.4]
, or more generally,
[
13
, Theorem 1]
,
there exists a constant

c_{1}

such that for any
x>0
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}>x\right)\leq c_{1}e^{-x^{2}/(c_{1}q)}.

(3.85)

Using (
3.83
), (
3.4
) and (
3.85
), we conclude that, when
i
is large enough, for all
q<t_{i}/2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbb{P}(D(\ell))\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right)\leq c_{1}e^{-C_{5}^{2}t_{i}/(32^{2}c_{1}q)}=:c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/q}.

(3.86)

Also, for
q=t_{i}/2-1
, we have that

\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}/2-1}\mathbb{P}(D(\ell))\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq t_{i}/2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right).

Recall the definition of
\rho_{l}
given by (
3.49
).
If
\sigma_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}>0
, then by Lemma
2.6
and the continuous mapping theorem,

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq t_{i}/2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i\to\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Pi_{0}\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1/2}\sigma_{-\lambda^{*}}B_{t}/(\lambda^{*}v^{*})\geq C_{5}/32\right)<1.

Otherwise, if
\sigma_{-\lambda^{*}}^{2}=0
, by Lemma
2.6
, when
C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32>\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq t_{i}/2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right)=0.

In conclusion,
there exists a constant

c_{3}\in(0,1)

such that for large
i
,

\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}/2-1}\mathbb{P}(D(\ell))\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq t_{i}/2}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}{\rho}_{\ell}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/32\right)\leq c_{3}.

(3.87)

For
i
so that
t_{i}<4(t_{i}/2-1)
, by (
3.86
) and (
3.87
), applying Abel’s equation
\sum_{k=1}^{q}a_{k}b_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{q-1}(a_{k}-a_{k+1})\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}b_{\ell}+a_{q}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}b_{\ell}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}/2-2}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}-\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(\ell+1)}\right\}\right)\sum_{q=1}^{\ell}\mathbb{P}(D(q))+\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(t_{i}/2-1)}\right\}\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}/2-1}\mathbb{P}(D(\ell))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}/2-2}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}-\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(\ell+1)}\right\}\right)c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/\ell}+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{4}{C_{9}}\right\}.

(3.88)

Noting that
(e^{a/x})^{\prime}=-ax^{-2}e^{a/x},
we have

\displaystyle\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}-\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}(\ell+1)}\right\}\right)c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/\ell}

\displaystyle=c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/\ell}\int_{\ell}^{\ell+1}\frac{\varepsilon t_{i}}{C_{9}x^{2}}e^{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}x}}\mathrm{d}x\leq c_{1}\int_{\ell}^{\ell+1}\frac{\varepsilon t_{i}}{C_{9}x^{2}}e^{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}x}}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/x}\mathrm{d}x.

So by (
3.4
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell)\right)\leq c_{1}\int_{1}^{t_{i}/2-1}\frac{\varepsilon t_{i}}{C_{9}x^{2}}e^{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}x}}e^{-c_{2}t_{i}/x}\mathrm{d}x+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{4}{C_{9}}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle z:=t_{i}/x}}{{=}}c_{1}\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{9}}\int_{t_{i}/(t_{i}/2-1)}^{t_{i}}e^{z(\varepsilon/C_{9}-c_{2})}\mathrm{d}z+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{4}{C_{9}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{9}}\int_{2}^{\infty}e^{z(\varepsilon/C_{9}-c_{2})}\mathrm{d}z+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{4}{C_{9}}\right\}=:F(\varepsilon).

(3.89)

Since
F(0)<1
,
we may take
i_{1}
sufficiently large and
\varepsilon
sufficiently small such that

F(\varepsilon)\cdot\frac{1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i_{1}}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}<1.

Since
\zeta
is decreasing, for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
and
i\geq i_{1}
, using (
3.4
) and (
3.4
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{i}-t_{i-1}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D(\ell)\right)\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\cdot F(\varepsilon)\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i_{1}}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\cdot F(\varepsilon)\right)^{k}=:K<\infty.

\Box

Using similar arguments,
we have the following lemmas
3.13
-
3.15
.
Since the arguments are similar, we will only sketch the proofs.

In the following,
\bar{C}
is a positive constant which will be specified later in the proof of Lemma
3.3
.
Define

k(n):=\left[\log_{2}\left([\bar{C}\ln n]^{2}\right)\right]
and

\displaystyle r_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle:=[\bar{C}\ln n],\qquad L_{n}^{1}:=\left\lceil\frac{C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n}{4M_{\rho}}\right\rceil,

\displaystyle s_{0}^{1}

\displaystyle:=t_{k(n)}\land\inf\left\{k\geq r_{0}^{1}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{0}^{1}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{0}^{1}-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},\quad r_{j+1}^{1}:=s_{j}^{1}+L_{n}^{1},

\displaystyle s_{j+1}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{k\geq r_{j+1}^{1}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{j+1}^{1}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{\ell}^{(r_{j+1}^{1}-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}\land\left(r_{j+1}^{1}+t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{k:s_{k}^{1}\geq t_{k(n)}\}.

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\max_{[\bar{C}\ln n]\leq r\leq 2t_{k(n)},0<k\leq[\bar{C}\ln n]/\alpha_{2}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}\overline{\rho}_{m}^{\left(\left[2^{-1}\bar{C}\ln n\right]\right)}\leq\frac{1}{8}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},

where
\alpha_{2}
is defined in (
3.65
).
Recall that
C_{9}
is the constant in Lemma
3.12
.

Lemma 3.13

(i)
When
n
is large enough, on
\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1}
,
we have that

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x\geq C_{5}[\bar{C}\ln n]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\Big\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 0,\ \forall k\mbox{ such that }[\bar{C}\ln n]+1\leq k\leq t_{k(n)},

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\beta_{t_{k(n)}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq C_{5}t_{k(n)}^{1/2}/2\,\Big|\,\beta_{[\bar{C}\ln n]}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}=x\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq C_{9}^{\mathfrak{K}^{1}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{1}}\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}(s_{j}^{1}-r_{j}^{1})}\right\}=:Y_{n}^{1}.

(3.90)

(ii)
There exist positive constants
\varepsilon
and
K=K(\varepsilon)

such that for
n
large enough,

\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}\right\}\right)\leq K.

Proof:

For
\ell<t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]
, define

D^{1}(\ell):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{r=1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{r}^{(-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}<\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n,\,\forall k\in[1,\ell),\,\sum_{r=1}^{\ell}\overline{\rho}_{r}^{(-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}.

Using the
\zeta
-mixing condition, similar to (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}^{1}-r_{j}^{1}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1,s_{k}-r_{k}=\ell_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}^{1}-r_{j}^{1}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right)\mathbb{P}\left(D^{1}(\ell_{k})\right)\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n/(8M_{\rho})\right)\right).

(3.91)

As variants of
\mathcal{A}_{j}
,
(\mathcal{A}_{j})^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{B}
in Lemma
3.12
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1}:=\left\{\beta_{s_{j}^{1}}^{1}-\beta_{2}^{1}\geq 4C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}\bigcap\bigcap_{k=r_{j}^{1}+1}^{s_{j}^{1}}\left\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{r_{j}^{1}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}^{1}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1})^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{k=s_{j}^{1}+1}^{r_{j+1}^{1}}\left\{\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{s_{j}^{1}}^{1}+\sum_{\ell=s_{j}^{1}+1}^{k}\rho_{\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},\quad\mathcal{B}^{1}:=\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{1}}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{1}-1}(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{1})^{\prime}\right).

Then repeating the arguments in Step 1 and Step 2 in the proof of Lemma
3.12
,
also note that
C_{5}[\bar{C}\ln n]/2\geq C_{5}t_{k(n)}^{1/2}/2
,
we get (
3.13
) .

Define

\mathfrak{J}^{1}:=\inf\{j:s_{j}^{1}-r_{j}^{1}\geq t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]\},

and

b_{n}:=t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n].

By repeating the argument of the proof of Lemma
3.12
(ii), we get that
when
n
is large enough so that

t_{k(n)}\leq 2\left(t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{C_{9}}\right)^{\varepsilon(k+1)}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}(t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n])}\right\}\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n/(8M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D^{1}(\ell)\right)\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{C_{9}}\right)^{\varepsilon(k+1)}e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n/(8M_{\rho})\right)\right)^{k}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{b_{n}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D^{1}(\ell)\right)\right)^{k}.

Since
b_{n}\sim t_{k(n)}
, we have
\sqrt{b_{n}}\sim t_{k(n)}^{1/2}\leq\bar{C}\ln n
.
Similar to (
3.86
) and (
3.87
), we have, for
q<b_{n}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\mathbb{P}(D^{1}(\ell))

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{k(n)}/q},

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{b_{n}-1}\mathbb{P}(D^{1}(\ell))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq b_{n}}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\rho_{m}\geq\frac{1}{8}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right)\leq c_{3}<1.

The same arguments as in (
3.4
) and (
3.4
) show that for large
n
such that
t_{k(n)}\leq 2(b_{n}-1)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{b_{n}-1}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left(D^{1}(\ell)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{b_{n}-2}\left(\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}\ell}\right\}-\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}(\ell+1)}\right\}\right)c_{1}e^{-c_{2}t_{k(n)}/\ell}+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}(b_{n}-1)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{9}}\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{z(\varepsilon/C_{9}-c_{2})}\mathrm{d}z+c_{3}\exp\left\{\varepsilon\frac{2}{C_{9}}\right\}=:F^{1}(\varepsilon).

Thus, there exists
n_{0}>0
such that when
n\geq n_{0}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}\right\}\right)\leq\frac{e^{2\varepsilon/C_{9}}}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n_{0}/(8M_{\rho})\right)\right)}{(C_{9})^{\varepsilon}}F^{1}(\varepsilon)\right)^{k}.

The rest of the proof is the same as the proof of (ii) in Lemma
3.12
.

\Box

Recall that
t_{i}=2^{i}
, and
L_{i}
is defined in (
3.4
). For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
define

\displaystyle r_{0}^{(2,n)}

\displaystyle:=t_{i-1},

\displaystyle s_{0}^{(2,n)}

\displaystyle:=t_{i}\land\inf\left\{k\geq r_{0}^{(2,n)}+1:\ \sum_{\ell=r_{0}^{(2,n)}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{i}/2]))}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\},\quad r_{j+1}^{(2,n)}:=s_{j}^{(2,n)}+L_{i},

\displaystyle s_{j+1}^{(2,n)}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{k\geq r_{j+1}^{(2,n)}+1:\ \sum_{\ell=r_{j+1}^{(2,n)}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}\land(r_{j+1}^{(2,n)}+t_{i}-t_{i-1}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{(2,n)}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{k:s_{k}^{(2,n)}\geq t_{i}\}.

Also define

\displaystyle G_{i}^{(2,n)}:=\left\{\max_{r\in[t_{i-1},t_{i+1}],0<k\leq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}\overline{\rho}_{n-m}^{(n-t_{i+2})}\leq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/32\right\},

where
\alpha_{1}
is defined in (
3.65
).

When considering
I_{2}
, we need to be more careful
since the definition of
\beta_{k}^{2}
only makes sense for
k\geq 1
. Note that our definition of
s_{j}^{(2,n)}
and
r_{j}^{(2,n)}
does not need any restriction for
n
. Thus, the proof of (ii)
for Lemma
3.14
below
is the same as Lemma
3.12
. Also note that our proof for (i) in Lemma
3.12
only relies on
the property that the sequence of independent random variables
H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left[H_{\ell}^{1}-H_{\ell-1}^{1}\right],\ell\geq 2
is a sequence of centered sub-exponential random variables satisfying (
3.46
) and (
3.48
).
The strong Markov property of
\Xi^{1}
only used for the independence of
H_{\ell}^{1}
.
Note that for
k\geq 2
, we have

\beta_{k}^{2}-\beta_{k-1}^{2}=-\rho_{k}+H_{k}^{2}-H_{k-1}^{2}-\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left[H_{k}^{2}-H_{k-1}^{2}\right]=:-\rho_{k}+\Delta H_{k}^{2},

and for
|\eta|<\gamma(-\lambda^{*})-(m-1)\textup{es}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(e^{\eta\Delta H_{k}^{2}}\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta\left(H_{k}^{2}+\frac{1}{v^{*}}\left(\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(k,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}-\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(k-1,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}{\psi(k-1,-\lambda^{*},\omega)}\right)\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k-1}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\exp\left\{\eta\left(H_{k}^{2}+\frac{1}{v^{*}}\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(1,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{k-1}\omega)}{\psi(1,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{k-1}\omega)}\right)\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{\frac{\eta}{v^{*}}\frac{\psi_{\lambda}(1,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{k-1}\omega)}{\psi(1,-\lambda^{*},\theta_{k-1}\omega)}\right\}\Pi_{0}\left(\exp\left\{\int_{0}^{H_{1}}\left((m-1)\xi(B_{s},\theta_{k-1}\omega)-\gamma(-\lambda^{*})+\eta\right)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\right)

(3.92)

and the right-hand side of (
3.4
) also makes sense for
k\leq 1
.
Therefore, to extend the case of
\beta_{k}^{2}
for
k\leq 1
,
we ignore the definition of
\beta_{0}^{2}
and
\beta_{1}^{2}
defined in (
3.18
), and
we take a sequence of independent random variables
\left\{\Delta H_{k}^{2}:k\leq 1\right\}
independent to everything else with Laplace function given in (
3.4
) under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
,
then, or
k\leq 1
, define

\beta_{k}^{2}:=\beta_{2}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{2}\left(\rho_{\ell}-\Delta H_{\ell}^{2}\right).

Replacing the definition of
\beta_{0}^{2}
and
\beta_{1}^{2}
defined in (
3.18
), by the above, Lemma
3.14
(i) can be proven by an argument similar to that of Lemma
3.12
(i).

Lemma 3.14

(i) For sufficiently large
i
and

n\geq 2^{i-1}
,
on
G_{i}^{(2,n)}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x\geq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/2}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\{\beta_{n-t_{i}}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq C_{5}{t_{i}}^{1/2}/2\}\cap\cap^{t_{i}}_{k=t_{i-1}+1}\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq t_{i}^{1/4}\}\Big|\beta_{n-t_{i-1}}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}=x\right)

\displaystyle\geq C_{9}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2,n)}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2,n)}}\frac{t_{i}}{C_{9}\left(s_{j}^{(2,n)}-r_{j}^{(2,n)}\right)}\right\}=:Z_{i}^{(2,n)},

(3.93)

here
C_{9}
is the constant in Lemma
3.12
.

(ii) There exist constants
\varepsilon>0
and

K=K(\varepsilon)
such that for
n
large enough,

\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}\ln Z_{i}^{(2,n)}\right\}\right)\leq K.

(3.94)

Proof:
(i) For
r<s
define

\displaystyle D^{(2,n)}(r,s):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\forall k\in[r+1,s),\sum_{\ell=r+1}^{s}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-s-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}.

(3.95)

Then by (
3.52
) we see that
D^{(2,n)}(r,s)\in\mathcal{F}_{n-r}\cap\mathcal{F}^{n-s-[L_{i}/2]}
. Define

D^{(2,n)}(\ell):=\left\{\omega:\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{i}/2])}<C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16,\forall k\in[1,s),\sum_{\ell=1}^{s}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-s-[L_{i}/2])}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\}.

Similar to (
3.4
) and (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}^{(2,n)}-r_{j}^{(2,n)}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1,s_{k}^{(2,n)}-r_{k}^{(2,n)}=\ell_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(s_{j}^{(2,n)}-r_{j}^{(2,n)}=\ell_{j},0\leq j\leq k-1\right)\mathbb{P}\left(D^{(2,n)}(\ell_{k})\right)\left(1+\zeta\left(C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/(32M_{\rho})\right)\right).

(3.96)

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2,n)}:=\left\{\beta_{n-s_{j}^{(2,n)}}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}\right\}\bigcap\bigcap^{s_{j}^{(2,n)}}_{k=r_{j}^{(2,n)}+1}\left\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n-r_{j}^{(2,n)}}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}^{(2,n)}+1}^{k}\rho_{n-\ell}\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\},

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2,n)}\right)^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{k=s_{j}^{(2,n)}+1}^{r_{j+1}^{(2,n)}}\left\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n-s_{j}^{(2,n)}}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=s_{j}^{(2,n)}+1}^{k}\rho_{n-\ell}\geq-C_{5}t_{i}^{1/2}/16\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(2,n)}:=\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2,n)}}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2,n)}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2,n)}-1}\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2,n)}\right)^{\prime}\right).

Then repeating the proof of Lemma
3.12
(i), we get (
3.14
).

(ii) Define

\mathfrak{J}^{(2,n)}:=\inf\{j:s_{j}^{(2,n)}-r_{j}^{(2,n)}\geq t_{i}-t_{i-1}\}.

Although
s_{j}^{(2,n)}-r_{j}^{(2,n)}
may depend on
n
, we see that the upper bound of (
3.86
) and (
3.87
) still hold if we replace
D(\ell_{k})
by
D^{(2,n)}(\ell_{k})
because of the stationarity of
\rho
.
Repeating the argument in the proof of Lemma
3.12
(ii), we get that (ii) holds.
The proof is now complete.

Let

L_{n}^{1}
be defined in Lemma

.
Define

\displaystyle r_{0}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=[\bar{C}\ln n],

\displaystyle s_{0}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=t_{k(n)}\land\inf\left\{k\geq r_{0}^{(2)}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{0}^{(2)}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},\quad r_{j+1}^{(2)}:=s_{j}^{(2)}+L_{n}^{1}

\displaystyle s_{j+1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{k\geq r_{j+1}^{(2)}+1:\sum_{\ell=r_{j+1}^{(2)}+1}^{k}\overline{\rho}_{n-\ell}^{(n-k-[L_{n}^{1}/2])}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}\land\left(r_{j+1}^{(2)}+t_{k(n)}-[\bar{C}\ln n]\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\inf\{k:s_{k}^{(2)}\geq t_{k(n)}\}.

\alpha_{2}

).

Lemma 3.15

(i) For sufficiently large
n
, on
\tilde{G}_{n}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{y\in[-1,1]}\inf_{x\geq C_{5}[\bar{C}\ln n]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left\{\{\beta_{n-t_{k(n)}}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq C_{5}t_{k(n)}^{1/2}/2\}\cap\cap^{t_{k(n)}}_{k=[\bar{C}\ln n]+1}\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq 0\}\mid\beta_{n-[\bar{C}\ln n]}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}=x\right\}

\displaystyle\geq C_{9}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2)}+1}\exp\left\{-\sum_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2)}}\frac{t_{k(n)}}{C_{9}\left(s_{j}^{(2)}-r_{j}^{(2)}\right)}\right\}=:Y_{n}^{2},

here

.

(ii) There exist

\varepsilon
and

such that for

Proof:
(i) Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2)}:=\left\{\beta_{n-s_{j}^{(2)}}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq 4C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}\bigcap\bigcap^{s_{j}^{(2)}}_{k=r_{j}^{(2)}+1}\left\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n-r_{j}^{(2)}}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=r_{j}^{(2)}+1}^{k}\rho_{n-\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2)}\right)^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{k=s_{j}^{(2)}+1}^{r_{j+1}^{(2)}}\left\{\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n-s_{j}^{(2)}}^{2}+\sum_{\ell=s_{j}^{(2)}+1}^{k}\rho_{n-\ell}\geq-\frac{1}{4}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(2)}:=\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2)}}\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2)}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{\mathfrak{K}^{(2)}-1}\left(\mathcal{A}_{j}^{(2)}\right)^{\prime}\right).

Proof of Lemma
3.3
:

Recall the definitions of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
in (
3.2
).
Recall that

k(n)=\left[\log_{2}\left([\bar{C}\ln n]^{2}\right)\right]
.
Define

j(n):=\lceil\log_{2}n\rceil
.
Let
\bar{C}
be a constant to be specified later.
By (
3.4
), there exists a large
N_{1}
such that for
i,n\geq N_{1}
and
\ell\geq t_{i+2}=2^{i+2}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{r\in[t_{i-1},t_{i+1}],0<k\leq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}{\rho}_{m}\leq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/64\right\}

\displaystyle\subset G_{i}^{1},

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{[\bar{C}\ln n]\leq r\leq 2[\bar{C}\ln n]^{2},0<k\leq[\bar{C}\ln n]/\alpha_{2}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}{\rho}_{m}\leq\frac{1}{16}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1},

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{r\in[t_{i-1},t_{i+1}],0<k\leq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}{\rho}_{\ell-m}\leq C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/64\right\}

\displaystyle\subset G_{i}^{(2,\ell)},

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{[\bar{C}\ln n]\leq r\leq 2[\bar{C}\ln n]^{2},0<k\leq[\bar{C}\ln n]/\alpha_{2}}\ \sum_{m=r+1}^{r+k}{\rho}_{n-m}\leq\frac{1}{16}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\widetilde{G}_{n}^{2}.

Take an integer
N_{2}
such that
N_{2}\geq 2^{N_{1}+2}
.
By Lemma
3.8
, (
3.85
) and the stationarity of
\rho_{m}
,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N_{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=k(n)}^{j(n)}\mathbb{P}\left((G_{i}^{1})^{c}\right)+\sum_{\ell=N_{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=k(\ell)}^{j(\ell)}\mathbb{P}\left((G_{i}^{(2,\ell)})^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=N_{2}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=k(n)}^{j(n)}t_{i+1}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{0\leq k\leq t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\ \sum_{m=1}^{k}\rho_{m}>C_{5}t_{i-1}^{1/2}/64\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=N_{2}}^{\infty}2n\sum_{i=k(n)}^{j(n)}c_{1}\exp\left\{-\alpha_{1}C_{5}^{2}t_{i-1}/(64^{2}c_{1}t_{i}^{1/2})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 4c_{1}\sum_{n=N_{2}}^{\infty}n\log_{2}(n)\exp\left\{-\alpha_{1}C_{5}^{2}\bar{C}\ln n/(64^{2}c_{1})\right\}<\infty,

(3.97)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N_{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}\right)^{c}\right)+\sum_{n=N_{1}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left(\tilde{G}_{n}^{2}\right)^{c}\right)\leq 2\sum_{n=N_{1}}^{\infty}2[\bar{C}\ln n]^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{0\leq k\leq[\bar{C}\ln n]/\alpha_{2}}\ \sum_{m=1}^{k}\rho_{m}>\frac{1}{16}C_{5}\bar{C}\ln n\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{n=N_{1}}^{\infty}[\bar{C}\ln n]^{2}\cdot c_{1}\exp\left\{-\alpha_{2}C_{5}^{2}[\bar{C}\ln n]/(16^{2}c_{1})\right\}<\infty,

(3.98)

by taking
\bar{C}
large enough.

Recall the definition of
Z_{i}^{1}
and

G_{i}^{1}

in Lemma
3.12
.
By (
3.55
), (
3.52
), we have that for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
large enough such that
t_{i-1}>L_{i}
,
t_{i}>t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}
and
t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]>t_{i-2}
,

\displaystyle G_{i}^{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-2}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}+t_{i}^{1/2}/\alpha_{1}}\subset\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-2}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}+t_{i}},

\displaystyle Z_{i}^{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-1}-[L_{i}/2]}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}}\subset\mathcal{F}^{t_{i-2}}\cap\mathcal{F}_{t_{i+1}+t_{i}}.

(3.99)

Then when
i
is large enough,
Z_{4i}^{1},G_{4i}^{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{t_{4i+1}+t_{4i}}\cap\mathcal{F}^{t_{4i-2}}
.
Together with Lemma
3.12
, for large
i
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{4i}^{1}}\ln Z_{4i}^{1}\right\}\Big|\mathcal{F}_{t_{4i-3}+t_{4i-4}}\right)\leq\left(1+\zeta(2^{4i-4})\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon 1_{G_{4i}^{1}}\ln Z_{4i}^{1}\right\}\right)\leq K\left(1+\zeta(0)\right).

Thus, we have, for
k=0,1,2,3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon\sum_{i=\lceil k(n)/4\rceil}^{[j(n)/4]-1}1_{G_{4i}^{1}}\ln Z_{4i+k}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=\lceil k(n)/4\rceil}^{[j(n)/4]-1}K\left(1+\zeta(0)\right)\leq c_{1}^{j(n)}

for some constant
c_{1}>0.
Now for a constant
c_{2}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}<-c_{2}j(n)\right)\leq\sum_{k=0}^{3}\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=\lceil k(n)/4\rceil}^{[j(n)/4]-1}1_{G_{4i+k}^{1}}\ln Z_{4i+k}<-c_{2}j(n)/4\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-\varepsilon c_{2}j(n)/4}\max_{k=1,2,3,4}\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left\{-\varepsilon\sum_{i=\lceil k(n)/4\rceil}^{[j(n)/4]-1}1_{G_{4i+k}^{1}}\ln Z_{4i+k}\right\}\right)\leq 4e^{-\varepsilon c_{2}j(n)/4}\cdot c_{1}^{j(n)}.

Letting
c_{2}
be large enough so that
-\varepsilon c_{2}/4+\ln c_{1}<-\ln 2
, there exists
N_{3}
such that

\sum_{n=N_{3}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}<-c_{2}j(n)\right)\leq\sum_{n=N_{3}}^{\infty}4e^{-\varepsilon c_{2}j(n)/4}\cdot c_{1}^{j(n)}<\infty.

(3.100)

Similarly, by Lemma
3.13
, Lemma
3.14
and Lemma
3.15
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N_{3}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}<-c_{2}\ln n\right)+\sum_{n=N_{3}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}\ln Z_{i}^{(2,n)}<-c_{2}j(n)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{n=N_{3}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{2}}\ln Y_{n}^{2}<-c_{2}\ln n\right)<\infty.

(3.101)

Now define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Omega}:=

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}\bigcap\left\{(G_{n}^{1})^{c}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}\bigcap\left\{(\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1})^{c}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}\bigcap\left\{\left(\bigcap_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}G_{i}^{(2,n)}\right)^{c}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}\bigcap\left\{(\widetilde{G}_{n}^{2})^{c}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}

\displaystyle\bigcap\left\{\left\{\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}<-c_{2}j(n)\right\}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}\bigcap\left\{\left\{1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}<-c_{2}\ln n\right\}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}

\displaystyle\bigcap\left\{\left\{\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}\ln Z_{i}^{(2,n)}<-c_{2}j(n)\right\}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}\bigcap\left\{\left\{1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{2}}\ln Y_{n}^{2}<-c_{2}\ln n\right\}\ \textup{i.o.}\right\}^{c}.

Then by (
3.4
), (
3.4
), (
3.100
) and (
3.4
),

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Omega})=1
.
By the construction of
\widetilde{\Omega}
, for every
\omega\in\widetilde{\Omega}
, there exists
N=N(\omega)
such that when
n\geq N
, we have

1_{G_{n}^{1}}=1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{1}}=\prod_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}=1_{\tilde{G}_{n}^{2}}=1,

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\geq-c_{2}j(n),\quad 1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}\geq-c_{2}\ln n,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}\ln Z_{i}^{(2,n)}\geq-c_{2}j(n),\quad 1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{2}}\ln Y_{n}^{2}\geq-c_{2}\ln n.

Now for
\omega\in\widetilde{\Omega}
and
n\geq N
, it holds that uniformly for
y\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{1}^{1}\geq 0,\ \forall 1\leq k\leq n,\beta_{n}^{1}-\beta_{1}^{1}\geq n^{1/4}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{k=1}^{[\bar{C}\ln n]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{k}^{1}-\beta_{k-1}^{1}>C_{5}\right)\cdot Y_{n}^{1}\cdot\prod_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}Z_{i}^{1}

\displaystyle\geq C_{5}^{[\bar{C}\ln n]}\cdot\exp\left\{1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{1}}\ln Y_{n}^{1}\right\}\cdot\exp\left\{\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{1}}\ln Z_{i}^{1}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq C_{5}^{[\bar{C}\ln n]}\cdot e^{-c_{2}\ln n}\cdot e^{-c_{2}j(n)}\geq\frac{1}{C_{5}}n^{\bar{C}\ln C_{5}-c_{2}-c_{2}/\ln 2},

where in the second inequality we used Lemma
3.8
.
Taking
[V_{t}]=n
, and
\ell^{\prime\prime}>-\bar{C}\ln C_{5}+c_{2}+c_{2}/\ln 2
,
we get the first result.

The second result can be proved similarly. Note that
n\geq 2^{j(n)-1}=t_{j(n)-1}
holds for every
n>2
. When
n\geq N(\omega)
, by Lemma
3.14
, uniformly for
y\in[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq 0,\ \forall 1\leq k\leq n,\beta_{2}^{2}-\beta_{n}^{2}\geq n^{1/4}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{k=1}^{[\bar{C}\ln n]}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\beta_{n-k}^{2}-\beta_{n-k+1}^{2}>C_{5}\right)\cdot Y_{n}^{2}\cdot\prod_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}Z_{i}^{(2,n)}

\displaystyle\geq C_{5}^{[\bar{C}\ln n]}\cdot\exp\left\{1_{\widetilde{G}_{n}^{2}}\ln Y_{n}^{2}\right\}\cdot\exp\left\{\sum_{i=k(n)+1}^{j(n)}1_{G_{i}^{(2,n)}}\ln Z_{i}^{(2,n)}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{C_{5}}n^{\bar{C}\ln C_{5}-c_{2}-c_{2}/\ln 2}.

Taking
[V_{t}]=n
and
\ell^{\prime\prime}>-\bar{C}\ln C_{5}+c_{2}+c_{2}/\ln 2
completes the proof.

\Box

4
Appendix

Lemma 4.1

For
j=1,2
, let
\beta_{k}^{j}
be define by (
3.18
). Under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}
,
\{\beta_{k}^{j},k\geq 1\}
has independent increments.

Proof:

We only need to prove the result for
j=1
.
For any
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k_{0}<k_{1}<...<k_{n}
, we only need to prove that
H_{k_{1}}^{1}-H_{k_{0}}^{1},...,H_{k_{n}}^{1}-H_{k_{n-1}}^{1}
are independent.
For any bounded measurable functions
f_{1},...,f_{n}
, by the strong Markov property of
\left\{\Xi_{t},t\geq 0;\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{H_{k_{0}}^{1}}\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\prod_{j=2}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{H_{k_{1}}^{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{2}\left(H_{k_{2}}^{1}\right)\prod_{j=3}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{2}\left(H_{k_{2}}^{1}\right)\prod_{j=3}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{2}\left(H_{k_{2}}^{1}\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\prod_{j=3}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{H_{k_{2}}^{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{0}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{1}\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}\right)\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{2}\left(H_{k_{2}}^{1}\right)\right)\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{2}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{3}\left(H_{k_{3}}^{1}\right)\prod_{j=4}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\cdots=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{j-1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}\right)\right).

Taking expectation in the display above, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{j-1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}\right)\right).

(4.1)

In particular, taking
f_{1}=...=f_{n-1}=1
, we get

\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{n}\left(H_{k_{n}}^{1}-H_{k_{n-1}}^{1}\right)\right)=\widetilde{\Pi}_{k_{n-1}}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{n}\left(H_{k_{n}}^{1}\right)\right).

Thus, (
4.1
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{\Pi}_{y}^{\xi,-\lambda^{*}}\left(f_{j}\left(H_{k_{j}}^{1}-H_{k_{j-1}}^{1}\right)\right),

(4.2)

which says that
\left(H_{k_{1}}^{1}-H_{k_{0}}^{1}\right),...,\left(H_{k_{n}}^{1}-H_{k_{n-1}}^{1}\right)
are independent.

\Box
